- [ ] **Step 4: The worker's handler, the worker, and prepaint**

Create `src/birds/paint/job.ts`:

```ts
import { getSpec } from '../species'
import { paintSpecies, type AtlasJob, type PaintedAtlas } from './painter'

/**
 * The paint worker's handler, a plain function so node tests can run it: paint one species-and-version atlas from
 * the registry, timed. The pixels own their whole buffer, so the worker can transfer it back rather than copy it.
 */
export function paintJob(job: AtlasJob): PaintedAtlas {
  const t0 = performance.now()
  const pixels = paintSpecies(getSpec(job.code), job.variant)
  return { code: job.code, variant: job.variant, pixels, ms: performance.now() - t0 }
}
```

Create `src/birds/paint/paint.worker.ts`. It type-checks under the project's DOM lib: `postMessage(message, { transfer })` is also the window's signature. The cast is there because TypeScript types a typed array's `.buffer` as `ArrayBufferLike`, which includes `SharedArrayBuffer`.

```ts
// The paint worker: an atlas job in, its pixels (transferred, not copied) and paint time out. main.tsx starts it
// with `new Worker(new URL(...), { type: 'module' })`, which Vite bundles as a worker of its own.
import { paintJob } from './job'
import type { AtlasJob } from './painter'

addEventListener('message', (e: MessageEvent<AtlasJob>) => {
  const reply = paintJob(e.data)
  postMessage(reply, { transfer: [reply.pixels.buffer as ArrayBuffer] })
})
```

Create `src/birds/paint/prepaint.ts`:

```ts
import type { Location } from '../../location/schema'
import type { SpeciesSpec } from '../spec'
import { SPECIES, getSpec } from '../species'
import { expandCast } from '../spawn'
import { makeRng } from '../../catalog/rng'
import { atlasKey, atlasReady, materialFor, storeAtlas, type AtlasJob, type PaintedAtlas } from './painter'

/**
 * Every atlas the cast's detailed birds can need: each version of a species with versions, and the species' own
 * plumage for a bird with none. That covers whatever versions a 'mixed' entry picks, so the list doesn't depend on
 * the spawner's seed. Flyover flocks are block birds and need none. `specs` is the registry, or a test's own.
 */
export function castAtlases(loc: Location, specs: Record<string, SpeciesSpec> = SPECIES): AtlasJob[] {
  const jobs = new Map<string, AtlasJob>()
  const add = (code: string, variant?: string) => { jobs.set(atlasKey({ code, variant }), { code, variant }) }
  for (const ind of expandCast(loc, makeRng(1))) {
    const spec = specs[ind.species]
    if (!spec?.detail) continue
    if (ind.variant === undefined) add(ind.species)
    else for (const v of Object.keys(spec.variants)) add(ind.species, v)
  }
  return [...jobs.values()]
}

/** The part of a Worker that prepaint uses, so a test can hand it a stand-in. */
export interface PaintWorker {
  onmessage: ((e: MessageEvent<PaintedAtlas>) => void) | null
  onerror: ((e: ErrorEvent) => void) | null
  postMessage(job: AtlasJob): void
  terminate(): void
}

export interface PrepaintOptions {
  /** makes the paint worker (main.tsx: `paint.worker.ts`); none, or one that throws, paints on the main thread */
  worker?: () => PaintWorker
  /** called at the start and after each atlas: how many are ready, of how many */
  onProgress?: (done: number, total: number) => void
}

/**
 * Paint every atlas in `jobs` that isn't painted yet, in the worker, and keep each for materialFor, so building a bird
 * paints nothing. Each paint's time goes into paintStats. If the worker can't be made, or fails, whatever it hasn't
 * painted is painted here on the main thread, one atlas per task so the page can still redraw between them.
 * Resolves once every atlas is ready.
 */
export async function prepaint(jobs: readonly AtlasJob[], opts: PrepaintOptions = {}): Promise<void> {
  const todo = new Map<string, AtlasJob>()
  for (const j of jobs) if (!atlasReady(j)) todo.set(atlasKey(j), j)
  const total = todo.size
  let done = 0
  opts.onProgress?.(0, total)
  if (total === 0) return

  let worker: PaintWorker | null = null
  try {
    worker = opts.worker?.() ?? null
  } catch (err) {
    console.warn(`paint worker: ${String(err)}; painting on the main thread`)
  }
  if (worker) {
    const w = worker
    await new Promise<void>(resolve => {
      w.onmessage = e => {
        if (!todo.delete(atlasKey(e.data))) return
        storeAtlas(e.data)
        opts.onProgress?.(++done, total)
        if (todo.size === 0) resolve()
      }
      w.onerror = e => {
        console.warn(`paint worker failed (${e.message || 'it did not load'}); painting the rest on the main thread`)
        resolve()
      }
      for (const j of todo.values()) w.postMessage(j)
    })
    w.onmessage = null
    w.onerror = null
    w.terminate()
  }
  for (const j of todo.values()) {
    await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 0))
    materialFor(getSpec(j.code), j.variant)
    opts.onProgress?.(++done, total)
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Bite checks**

Each break below was made in the scratch worktree, `prepaint.test.ts` was run, and the file was restored:
- `storeAtlas` doesn't cache: 3 tests fail. Among them is "leaves materialFor nothing to paint", because materialFor paints again.
- `storeAtlas` doesn't call `tally`: 2 fail, on the `paintStats` deltas.
- `castAtlases` adds only the version the bird drew, not all of them: "paints every version …" fails.
- `onerror` never resolves: the worker-failure test times out at 5 s.
- No main-thread fallback loop: 3 fail.
- The fallback doesn't yield between atlases: "… one atlas per task so the page can redraw between them" fails.
- Already-painted atlases aren't skipped: "skips atlases already painted …" fails.

Compare pixel arrays by identity through `same(…)`, never with `expect(array).toBe(array)`. A failing `toBe` on two 1 MB arrays makes vitest diff them, and the run hangs; the first bite check here did exactly that.

- [ ] **Step 8: Write the failing title card test**

Create `src/ui/Title.test.tsx`. `Title` is a plain function component with no hooks, so the test calls it and walks the returned tree; it needs no DOM.

```tsx
import type { VNode } from 'preact'
import { Title, type TitleProps } from './Title'

/** Every element in a rendered tree, depth first; Title is a plain function of its props, so no DOM is needed. */
function elements(node: unknown): VNode<Record<string, unknown>>[] {
  if (Array.isArray(node)) return node.flatMap(elements)
  if (!node || typeof node !== 'object' || !('props' in node)) return []
  const v = node as VNode<Record<string, unknown>>
  return [v, ...elements(v.props.children)]
}
const text = (v: VNode<Record<string, unknown>>): string => {
  const c = v.props.children
  return (Array.isArray(c) ? c : [c]).join('')
}

const props = (painting: TitleProps['painting'], unsupported: string | null = null): TitleProps => ({
  unsupported, painting, onBegin: () => {}, onSettings: () => {}, onCredits: () => {},
})
const begin = (p: TitleProps) => elements(Title(p)).find(v => v.props.class === 'begin')
const status = (p: TitleProps) => elements(Title(p)).find(v => v.props.role === 'status')

describe('Title', () => {
  it('keeps Begin disabled while the birds are painting, and says how far along it is', () => {
    const p = props({ done: 7, total: 20, ready: false })
    expect(begin(p)!.props.disabled).toBe(true)
    expect(status(p)!.props.class).toBe('painting')
    expect(text(status(p)!)).toBe('Painting the birds… 7 of 20')
  })
  it('enables Begin once every atlas is ready, and hides the progress line but keeps its place, so Begin stays put', () => {
    const p = props({ done: 20, total: 20, ready: true })
    expect(begin(p)!.props.disabled).toBe(false)
    expect(status(p)!.props.class).toBe('painting ready')
  })
  it('shows neither Begin nor progress when the scene cannot render', () => {
    const p = props({ done: 0, total: 20, ready: false }, 'no WebGL')
    expect(begin(p)).toBeUndefined()
    expect(status(p)).toBeUndefined()
  })
})
```

In `src/main.tsx`, make these four replacements. Tasks 1–12 edit `main.tsx` only around the targeting candidates in the frame loop, so all four apply verbatim.

4. Replace

```tsx
world.add(spawnCast(loc, { registry, wind: world.wind, ground: (x, z) => surface.sample(x, z), ripples, seed: 42, events: spawnEvents, perches }))
```

with the block below. If an earlier task changed `spawnCast`'s arguments, keep that task's arguments inside the `.then`.

```tsx
// Every atlas the cast needs paints in a worker behind the title card. Only then are the birds built and Begin
// enabled, so no detailed bird is ever built before its atlas is ready.
prepaint(castAtlases(loc), {
  worker: () => new Worker(new URL('./birds/paint/paint.worker.ts', import.meta.url), { type: 'module' }),
  onProgress: (done, total) => painting.set({ done, total }),
}).then(() => {
  world.add(spawnCast(loc, { registry, wind: world.wind, ground: (x, z) => surface.sample(x, z), ripples, seed: 42, events: spawnEvents, perches }))
  painting.set({ ready: true })
}).catch(err => {
  console.error(err)
  renderApp('The birds could not be set up, so the lake cannot start. The credits still work.')
})
```

`renderApp(null)` stays where it is, after this block. `prepaint` reports `0 of N` synchronously before its first `await`, so the card's first render already shows the count. The frame loop, `calls`, and targeting all run with an empty registry until the birds are added. While the title is open the world is paused but still renders, so the birds' textures upload behind the card before Begin.

```ts
// src/birds/species/swimmer.testkit.ts: what every swimmer's species test uses
export type MM = Record<keyof Measures, number>      // millimetres; billWidestAt and billWidestRatio stay fractions
export const within: (v: number, lo: number, hi: number, what?: string) => void
export const mean: (xs: number[]) => number
export function built(spec: SpeciesSpec, variant?: string): { model: DetailedBirdModel; M: MM }
export const draftShare: (M: MM) => number           // draft / (backAbove + draft)
export function hsl(rgb: [number, number, number]): { hue: number; sat: number; lum: number }
export function atlas(spec: SpeciesSpec, variant?: string): {
  px; u(label): number; stats(part, s, t); colour(part, s, t); share(part, s, t, test): number
}
```

The traced bird measures (research section 10's bound in brackets):

| Measure | Built |
|---|---|

| Culmen | 53.0 mm (48–60) |

| Breast front above the water | 9.2 mm (the photo's 9.3) |
| Waterline length | 251.9 mm (250–335) |

- [ ] **Step 1: The shared test kit**

Create `src/birds/species/swimmer.testkit.ts`:

```ts
import { parseSpeciesSpec, type SpeciesSpec } from '../spec'
import { buildDetailedBird, type DetailedBirdModel } from '../detail/build'
import { measure, type Measures } from '../detail/measure'
import { createProfile } from '../detail/profile'
import { layersFor, type Part } from '../detail/spec'
import { paintAtlas, regionStats } from '../paint/painter'
import { REGIONS, pixelST, ATLAS_SIZE } from '../paint/atlas'

/**
 * Helpers the swimmers' species tests share: a built bird with its measures in millimetres, and a painted atlas with
 * ways to read it. Bounds live in each species' test file, from its research document's section 10.
 */

/** A bird's measures in millimetres, except the bill's widest point and width ratio, which stay fractions. */
export type MM = Record<keyof Measures, number>
const FRACTIONS: readonly string[] = ['billWidestAt', 'billWidestRatio']

export const within = (v: number, lo: number, hi: number, what = '') => {
  expect(v, what).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(lo)
  expect(v, what).toBeLessThanOrEqual(hi)
}

export const mean = (xs: number[]) => xs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / xs.length

/** A species built at rest in one plumage version, with its measures in millimetres. */
export function built(spec: SpeciesSpec, variant?: string): { model: DetailedBirdModel; M: MM } {
  const model = buildDetailedBird(parseSpeciesSpec(spec), variant)
  const M = Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(measure(model)).map(([k, v]) => [k, FRACTIONS.includes(k) ? v : v * 1000])) as MM
  return { model, M }
}

/** Fraction of the body's depth (top of the back to the keel) that rides under the water. */
export const draftShare = (M: MM) => M.draft / (M.backAbove + M.draft)

/** Hue in degrees (0..360), saturation and luminance (0..1) of an [r, g, b] colour, each 0..1. */
export function hsl([r, g, b]: [number, number, number]): { hue: number; sat: number; lum: number } {
  const max = Math.max(r, g, b), min = Math.min(r, g, b), l = (max + min) / 2, d = max - min
  const sat = d === 0 ? 0 : d / (1 - Math.abs(2 * l - 1))
  let hue = 0
  if (d > 0) {
    if (max === r) hue = 60 * (((g - b) / d) % 6)
    else if (max === g) hue = 60 * ((b - r) / d + 2)
    else hue = 60 * ((r - g) / d + 4)
  }
  return { hue: (hue + 360) % 360, sat, lum: 0.2126 * r + 0.7152 * g + 0.0722 * b }
}

/**
 * One plumage version's atlas, and ways to read it: `stats` is `regionStats` (mean luminance, spread, roughness, mean
 * rgb) over a part's (s, t) window; `u(label)` is a station's position along the skin's s; `share(part, s, t, test)`
 * is the fraction of that window's pixels passing `test`.
 */
export function atlas(spec: SpeciesSpec, variant?: string) {
  const s0 = parseSpeciesSpec(spec)
  const d = s0.detail!
  const profile = createProfile(d.profile)
  const px = paintAtlas({ code: s0.code, detail: d, layers: layersFor(d, variant), profile })
  const u = (label: string) => profile.uOf(label)
  const stats = (part: Part, s: [number, number], t: [number, number]) => regionStats(px, part, s, t)
  const colour = (part: Part, s: [number, number], t: [number, number]) => hsl(stats(part, s, t).rgb)
  const share = (part: Part, s: [number, number], t: [number, number], test: (c: ReturnType<typeof hsl>) => boolean) => {
    const r = REGIONS[part]
    let n = 0, yes = 0
    for (let y = r.y; y < r.y + r.h; y++) {
      for (let x = r.x; x < r.x + r.w; x++) {
        const [ps, pt] = pixelST(part, x, y)
        if (ps < s[0] || ps > s[1] || pt < t[0] || pt > t[1]) continue
        const k = (y * ATLAS_SIZE + x) * 4
        n++
        if (test(hsl([px[k]! / 255, px[k + 1]! / 255, px[k + 2]! / 255]))) yes++
      }
    }
    return n ? yes / n : 0
  }
  return { px, u, stats, colour, share }
}

/** A hue within [lo, hi] degrees, wrapping through 0 when lo > hi (e.g. 330..20 for reds). */
export const hueIn = (hue: number, lo: number, hi: number) => (lo <= hi ? hue >= lo && hue <= hi : hue >= lo || hue <= hi)
```

It is not a test file (Vitest collects `*.test.ts` only), and it uses the global `expect`, which the project's
`tsconfig.json` types.

```ts
import * as THREE from 'three'
import { SPEC as MALLARD } from './mallar3'
import { parseSpeciesSpec } from '../spec'
import { billWidthAt, billDepthAt } from '../detail/measure'
import { eyePlacement, billCenter } from '../detail/bill'

import { MALLARD as DABBLING } from '../../behavior/dabbler'
import { built, atlas, within, draftShare, hueIn } from './swimmer.testkit'

// Bounds from docs/research/2026-09-25-mallard.md, section 10 (Söderquist et al. 2014, Baird et al. 1884, AVONET, the
// NHMO skin, photo-measured), in mm, at rest with the waterline at y = 0. One profile, traced at the mean of the sexes,
// serves all three versions; only the drake's curl and the paint differ.

const D = parseSpeciesSpec(MALLARD).detail!
const A = built(MALLARD, 'male')
const VERSIONS = ['male', 'maleMoult', 'female'] as const
const bone = (m: typeof A.model, name: string) => m.mesh.skeleton.bones.find(b => b.name === name)!

describe('Mallard: structure', () => {
  it('builds every version as the same bird', () => {
    for (const v of ['maleMoult', 'female']) expect(built(MALLARD, v).M.length).toBeCloseTo(A.M.length, 6)
  })
  it('a long, flat bill: culmen, and width and depth at the base', () => {
    within(A.M.culmen, 48, 60)
    within(billWidthAt(A.model, 0) * 1000, 19, 24)
    within(billDepthAt(A.model, 0) * 1000, 15.5, 21)
    within((billWidthAt(A.model, 0) * 1000) / A.M.culmen, 0.36, 0.46)
  })
  it('nearly parallel-sided: narrowest a fifth of the way out, widest 55-80% out at 1.02-1.10 times the base', () => {
    expect(billWidthAt(A.model, 0.2)).toBeLessThan(billWidthAt(A.model, 0))
    within(A.M.billWidestAt, 0.55, 0.8)
    within(A.M.billWidestRatio, 1.02, 1.1)
  })
  it('deep at the base and a third to a half as deep at 80%, the culmen straight to the nail', () => {
    within(billDepthAt(A.model, 0.8) / billDepthAt(A.model, 0), 0.3, 0.55)
    within(A.M.billTipRise, -1.5, 1.5)
  })
  it('tarsus, middle toe, wing chord, tail, and length at rest', () => {
    within(A.M.tarsus, 36, 48)
    within(D.measurements.toe * 1000, 45, 57)
    within(A.M.wingChord, 250, 305)
    within(A.M.tail, 75, 105)
    within(A.M.length, 320, 400)
  })

  })

    within(A.M.backAbove, 70, 90)
    within(A.M.crownAbove, 85, 115)
    within(A.M.tailTipAbove, 20, 50)
    within(-A.M.projection, 0, 35)
    within(A.M.waterlineLength, 250, 335)
    within(A.M.draft, 40, 70)
    within(draftShare(A.M), 0.35, 0.48)

  it("in its own full tip-up the stern stands 0.10-0.15 m out of the water, and the feet paddle above it", () => {
    const m = built(MALLARD, 'female').model
    const p = defaultPose()
    p.lean.x = DABBLING.tipUp.lean; p.tailTilt = DABBLING.tipUp.tail; p.neckExtend = 1; p.paddle = 1
    m.pose(p)

    }

    within(curl.top.y * 1000, 45, 65)

})

describe('Mallard: plumage', () => {
  const m = atlas(MALLARD, 'male'), mm = atlas(MALLARD, 'maleMoult'), f = atlas(MALLARD, 'female')
  type Atlas = typeof m
  const green = (c: { hue: number; sat: number }) => hueIn(c.hue, 70, 170) && c.sat > 0.25
  const white = (c: { sat: number; lum: number }) => c.lum > 0.8 && c.sat < 0.25
  const head = (a: Atlas, t: [number, number]) => a.share('skin', [a.u('nape'), a.u('lores')], t, green)
  // the ring runs obliquely across the neck's skin, from the side of the neck behind to the throat
  const ring = (a: Atlas) => a.share('skin', [a.u('breast'), a.u('neckMid')], [0.3, 0.95], white)
  const flank = (a: Atlas) => a.stats('skin', [a.u('hip'), a.u('flank')], [0.42, 0.58])

  it('male: a green head over a narrow white ring', () => {
    expect(head(m, [0, 0.85])).toBeGreaterThan(0.6)
    within(ring(m), 0.02, 0.3)
  })
  it('male: dark at both ends and light in the middle: chestnut breast, pale grey flank, black stern', () => {
    const breast = m.colour('skin', [m.u('breast'), m.u('neckBase')], [0.6, 0.95])
    expect(hueIn(breast.hue, 0, 30)).toBe(true)
    expect(breast.sat).toBeGreaterThan(0.25)

    expect(side.sat).toBeLessThan(0.15)
    expect(side.lum).toBeGreaterThan(0.55)
    const stern = m.stats('skin', [m.u('rear'), m.u('rump')], [0.75, 1]).mean
    expect(stern).toBeLessThan(0.12)
    expect(side.lum).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2 * breast.lum)
    expect(side.lum).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2 * stern)
  })
  it("the drakes' bills are yellow; the female's orange, with a dark saddle on the upper third from about 30 to 50% out", () => {
    for (const a of [m, mm]) expect(hueIn(a.colour('bill', [0.2, 0.8], [0.6, 1]).hue, 42, 70)).toBe(true)
    const lower = (s: [number, number]) => f.stats('bill', s, [0.67, 1]).mean

  })
  it('female: contrasty flanks, dark-centred feathers with buff edges', () => {
    expect(flank(f).std).toBeGreaterThan(0.1)
    expect(flank(f).std).toBeGreaterThan(flank(m).std + 0.05)

  it('the moulting drake: green only on the crown and nape, over a grey-brown face; no ring', () => {
    expect(head(mm, [0, 0.2])).toBeGreaterThan(0.4)
    expect(head(mm, [0.4, 0.85])).toBeLessThan(0.05)

    expect(flank(mm).mean).toBeLessThan(flank(m).mean)
    expect(flank(mm).mean).toBeGreaterThan(flank(f).mean)
  })
})
```

```ts
import type { SpeciesSpec } from '../spec'
import type { Layer } from '../detail/spec'

// The three late-September versions share one traced bird and differ in paint (and the drake's curl). Colours are
// the research's words (docs/research/2026-09-25-mallard.md, sections 4 and 9), picked for a field-guide plate from
// the credited photos. Skin t: 0 the back's midline, about 0.2 the top of the side, 0.5 the widest, 0.55-0.6 the
// water along the flank (0.72 at the stern), 1 the belly.

/** The drakes' stern: black above and below back to the tail base, the white outer tail feathers along its side. */
const STERN: Layer[] = [
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rear', to: 'tailBase' }, color: '#141414', soft: 6 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rear', to: 'rump', band: [0.42, 0.56] }, color: '#efece5', soft: 1.5 },
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'rump', t: 0.49 }, to: { at: 'rump', t: 0.52, dx: 14 }, width: 4, color: '#efece5', blur: 1.5 },
]

/** The drakes' silvery flank, finely vermiculated, and the pale grey-brown scapulars along the top of the side. */
const GREY_BODY: Layer[] = [
  { kind: 'base', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rear', to: 'billBase' }, top: '#8f806c', side: '#d8d4cc', bottom: '#e4e0d8' },
  { kind: 'bars', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'tailBase', to: 'breast', band: [0.3, 1] }, color: '#8c877e', width: 0.4, spacing: 1.2, angle: 90, blur: 0.3, opacity: 0.45 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'tailBase', to: 'breast', band: [0, 0.3] }, color: '#b3a590', soft: 4 },
  { kind: 'bars', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'tailBase', to: 'breast', band: [0, 0.3] }, color: '#7a6d5c', width: 0.4, spacing: 1.2, angle: 90, blur: 0.3, opacity: 0.5 },
  // the mantle, dark brown where the back meets the neck
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'flank', to: 'neckBase', band: [0, 0.4] }, color: '#5e4531', soft: 8 },
]

/** A drake's chestnut breast, from the ring down to the water, its rear edge level with the front of the folded wing. */
const CHESTNUT: Layer[] = [
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'breast', to: 'neckMid', band: [0.33, 1] }, color: '#5a2819', soft: 4 },
]

/** The folded wing: the lower scapulars, the tertials with their chestnut outer webs, and the dark primary tips. */
const DRAKE_WING: Layer[] = [
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold' }, color: '#b8ab98' },
  { kind: 'bars', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.25, 0.75] }, color: '#857a6b', width: 0.4, spacing: 1.2, angle: 90, blur: 0.3, opacity: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.25, 0.76], t: [0.62, 1] }, color: '#5e3319', soft: 2 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.74, 0.9] }, color: '#8a7457', soft: 2 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.74, 0.88], t: [0.55, 1] }, color: '#6b4a2e', soft: 2 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.88, 1] }, color: '#2c2724', soft: 3 },
]

/** The spread wing, seen only if it flies: grey-brown, the speculum purplish blue between white bars. */
const SPREAD: Layer[] = [
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingSpread' }, color: '#8a7d6c' },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingSpread', s: [0.1, 0.45], t: [0.62, 0.92] }, color: '#3b3f9c', soft: 1.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingSpread', s: [0.1, 0.45], t: [0.56, 0.62] }, color: '#f2f0ea', soft: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingSpread', s: [0.1, 0.45], t: [0.92, 1] }, color: '#f2f0ea', soft: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingUnder' }, color: '#e6e2da' },
]

/** The drakes' tail: ash grey, its outer feathers white. */
const DRAKE_TAIL: Layer[] = [
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'tail' }, color: '#f0eee8' },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'tail', t: [0.3, 0.7] }, color: '#9e9990', soft: 2 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'tailUnder' }, color: '#ece9e2' },
]

const MALE: Layer[] = [
  ...GREY_BODY,
  ...STERN,
  ...CHESTNUT,
  // the chestnut's pale autumn fringes
  { kind: 'feathers', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'breast', to: 'neckBase', band: [0.4, 1] }, length: 6, width: 5, center: '#5a2819', edge: '#8a5a44', edgeWidth: 0.5, soft: 3, opacity: 0.5 },
  // the head: metallic green, blackish on the crown, brightest on the cheek; the green runs down the neck to the ring
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'neckMid', to: 'billBase' }, color: '#1f5a2e', soft: 1 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'neckBase', to: 'neckMid', band: [0, 0.62] }, color: '#1f5a2e', soft: 1 },
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'neckBase', t: 0.3, dx: -7 }, to: { at: 'neckBase', t: 0.61, dx: 7.8 }, width: 12, color: '#1f5a2e', blur: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'neckBase', t: 0.63, dx: 5 }, to: { at: 'neckMid', t: 1, dx: 1 }, width: 6, color: '#1f5a2e', blur: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'nape', to: 'lores', band: [0.35, 0.8] }, color: '#2c7432', soft: 3 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'occiput', to: 'lores', band: [0, 0.2] }, color: '#143b24', soft: 2 },
  // the ring, from the side of the neck (incomplete behind, under the mantle) round to the throat
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'neckBase', t: 0.3, dx: -13.5 }, to: { at: 'neckBase', t: 0.61, dx: 1.3 }, width: 2.5, color: '#f2f1ec', blur: 0.4 },
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'neckBase', t: 0.61, dx: 1.3 }, to: { at: 'neckMid', t: 0.97, dx: -3 }, width: 2.5, color: '#f2f1ec', blur: 0.4 },
  ...DRAKE_WING,
  ...SPREAD,
  ...DRAKE_TAIL,
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'curl' }, color: '#121614' },
  // brilliant lemon yellow to greenish yellow; the nail black, with a pale bluish sheen behind it
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill' }, color: '#dcc23a' },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.82, 0.9], t: [0, 0.4] }, color: '#a3ab86', soft: 1, opacity: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.9, 1], t: [0, 0.45] }, color: '#1b1a17', soft: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'legs' }, color: '#e8662a' },
]

/** A drake part-way out of eclipse, as the local birds show it: the body nearly done, the head lagging. */
const MALE_MOULT: Layer[] = [
  ...GREY_BODY,
  // brown scalloped feathers scattered through the grey flank
  { kind: 'feathers', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'tailBase', to: 'breast', band: [0.32, 0.8] }, length: 9, width: 8, center: '#8b6c4c', edge: '#cdbba2', edgeWidth: 1.2, soft: 3, opacity: 0.35 },
  ...STERN,
  ...CHESTNUT,
  // the chestnut mottled with brown, pale-fringed
  { kind: 'feathers', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'breast', to: 'neckMid', band: [0.35, 1] }, length: 7, width: 6, center: '#5c2d1c', edge: '#7a5a44', edgeWidth: 1, soft: 3 },
  // the head speckled grey-brown, a dark crown and line through the eye, green only on the crown and nape
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'neckBase', to: 'billBase', band: [0, 1] }, color: '#7c6a57', soft: 3 },
  { kind: 'spots', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'neckBase', to: 'billBase' }, color: '#3e3226', size: 0.9, spacing: 1.8, blur: 0.3, soft: 2, opacity: 0.8 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'occiput', to: 'lores', band: [0, 0.22] }, color: '#2d2820', soft: 1.5 },
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'lores', t: 0.45 }, to: { at: 'occiput', t: 0.36, dx: -3 }, width: 2, color: '#3a3026', blur: 0.6 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'nape', to: 'eye', band: [0, 0.2] }, color: '#1d5530', soft: 1.5, opacity: 0.9 },
  ...DRAKE_WING,
  { kind: 'feathers', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.3, 0.74], t: [0, 0.6] }, length: 9, width: 8, center: '#9a8a74', edge: '#c9bda9', edgeWidth: 1, soft: 2, opacity: 0.5 },
  ...SPREAD,
  ...DRAKE_TAIL,
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'curl' }, color: '#121614' },
  // yellow to olive-yellow, the nail dark; legs dull orange
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill' }, color: '#bfae3c' },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.9, 1], t: [0, 0.45] }, color: '#23211c', soft: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'legs' }, color: '#c9743a' },
]

/** The adult female, standing in for the juveniles too: mottled brown, a striped head, an orange bill with a saddle. */
const FEMALE: Layer[] = [
  { kind: 'base', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rear', to: 'billBase' }, top: '#5b4633', side: '#937758', bottom: '#cdb58f' },
  // bold dark-centred feathers with broad buff edges, darker above
  { kind: 'feathers', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rear', to: 'breast', band: [0.28, 1] }, length: 9, width: 8, center: '#3d2c1e', edge: '#cfae7c', edgeWidth: 1.7, soft: 3 },
  { kind: 'feathers', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rump', to: 'neckMid', band: [0, 0.3] }, length: 9, width: 8, center: '#2e2218', edge: '#b8966a', edgeWidth: 1.1, soft: 3 },
  // the undertail and the tail's whitish outer feathers along the stern
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rear', to: 'rump', band: [0.42, 0.56] }, color: '#e9e1d2', soft: 1.5 },
  { kind: 'streaks', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'rear', to: 'rump', band: [0.56, 1] }, color: '#6e5a44', length: 2, width: 0.6, spacing: 1.8, blur: 0.3, soft: 2, opacity: 0.7 },
  // the breast brown, spotted and streaked darker
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'breast', to: 'neckMid', band: [0.3, 1] }, color: '#a4845c', soft: 4 },
  { kind: 'spots', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'breast', to: 'neckMid', band: [0.3, 1] }, color: '#4a3422', size: 1.4, spacing: 2.6, blur: 0.3, soft: 3 },
  // the head: buff streaked brown, a dark crown and a dark line through the eye, paler cheeks, a plain buff throat
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'neckMid', to: 'billBase' }, color: '#b69c77', soft: 2 },
  { kind: 'streaks', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'neckMid', to: 'billBase', band: [0, 0.8] }, color: '#5a4630', length: 1.4, width: 0.4, spacing: 1.2, blur: 0.2, soft: 2, opacity: 0.7 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'nape', to: 'lores', band: [0, 0.22] }, color: '#46362a', soft: 1.5 },
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'lores', t: 0.3 }, to: { at: 'occiput', t: 0.27, dx: -3 }, width: 1.8, color: '#d9c7a6', blur: 0.6, opacity: 0.7 },
  { kind: 'stripe', from: { at: 'billBase', t: 0.5 }, to: { at: 'occiput', t: 0.38, dx: -4 }, width: 1.8, color: '#3d2e21', blur: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'skin', from: 'neckMid', to: 'billBase', band: [0.82, 1] }, color: '#dbc8a4', soft: 2 },
  // the folded wing: plain grey-brown tertials over dark-centred scapulars, and the rear white bar of the speculum
  // showing as a fleck at the wing's lower edge (the flank feathers hide the rest)
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold' }, color: '#7b6a58' },
  { kind: 'feathers', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.25, 0.74] }, length: 9, width: 8, center: '#3a2b1e', edge: '#c3a276', edgeWidth: 1.5, soft: 2 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.88, 1] }, color: '#3a322b', soft: 3 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.57, 0.63], t: [0.7, 0.8] }, color: '#3b4aa0', soft: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'wingFold', s: [0.57, 0.63], t: [0.8, 1] }, color: '#f2f0ea', soft: 0.5 },
  ...SPREAD,
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'tail' }, color: '#e4dccb' },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'tail', t: [0.3, 0.7] }, color: '#8c7b66', soft: 2 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'tailUnder' }, color: '#e4dccb' },
  // orange, with a dark saddle on the culmen from a tenth of the way out to four-fifths, deepest (half the side) at
  // 35-55%; dark spots on the basal third; the nail's tip dusky
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill' }, color: '#d98a2b' },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.1, 0.25], t: [0, 0.22] }, color: '#3a3026', soft: 1 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.25, 0.35], t: [0, 0.36] }, color: '#3a3026', soft: 1 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.35, 0.55], t: [0, 0.46] }, color: '#3a3026', soft: 1 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.55, 0.65], t: [0, 0.37] }, color: '#3a3026', soft: 1 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.65, 0.75], t: [0, 0.32] }, color: '#3a3026', soft: 1 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.75, 0.82], t: [0, 0.2] }, color: '#3a3026', soft: 1, opacity: 0.7 },
  { kind: 'spots', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.05, 0.33], t: [0.3, 0.6] }, color: '#3a3026', size: 1.2, spacing: 3, blur: 0.3, opacity: 0.8 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'bill', s: [0.96, 1], t: [0, 0.5] }, color: '#4a3a2c', soft: 0.5 },
  { kind: 'fill', where: { part: 'legs' }, color: '#e0773a' },
]

  code: 'mallar3',
  commonName: 'Mallard',
  length: 0.58,

    },
  },
  marks: {
    size: 'a large, heavy dabbling duck, the biggest on the lake, sitting long and level',
    bill: 'a long, broad, flat bill: yellow on drakes, olive-yellow on a drake still moulting, orange with a dark saddle on females',
    head: 'drakes: glossy green over a thin white ring; a drake still moulting is speckled brown with green only on the crown and nape; females buff with a dark crown and a dark line through the eye',
    body: 'drakes: chestnut breast, pale grey sides, a black stern with black curls over a white-edged tail; females mottled brown with a whitish tail. Upends to feed, tail in the air',
    legs: 'orange feet, seen when it upends',
  },
  wingbeatHz: 6,
  foraging: 'dabbler',
  detail: {
    measurements: {
      length: 0.358, culmen: 0.053, billDepth: 0.0183, billWidth: 0.021,
      tarsus: 0.043, tibia: 0.01, toe: 0.051, wingChord: 0.28, tail: 0.087,
    },
    // traced from Stan Bousson's drake (see sources; 4296 px, facing right) at 0.1058 mm per pixel (the 53 mm culmen,
    // the mean of the sexes, spans 501 px), hip at (1500, 1640). The photo's outline was read by hand. Each station's
    // up, down, and tilt, and the neck and head stations' places, were fitted so that the loft's side view (its own 64
    // rings) covers the outline with the least area between them, the loft never folds, and the eye lands on the
    // photo's eye. Under the water, where no photo shows the hull, the outline is a keel 60 mm down, from where the water
    // meets the stern to where it meets the breast. The neck base sits at the top of the breast's bulge, so the breast
    // is body, not neck. The loft lies within 1 mm of the photo along the crown and the back of the head, and on
    // average within 1.5 mm along the back, the nape, the forehead, the chin, the breast, and the stern; at worst the
    // chin falls 2.6 mm short under the bill, and the rump stands 3 mm above the photo's under the tertials, where the
    // wing covers it
    profile: [
      { label: 'rear',     x: -0.1222, y: 0.0132, up: 0.0010, down: 0.0097, halfWidth: 0.0080 },
      { label: 'rump',     x: -0.0994, y: 0.0101, up: 0.0325, down: 0.0180, halfWidth: 0.0300 },
      { label: 'tailBase', x: -0.0634, y: 0.0000, up: 0.0500, down: 0.0382, halfWidth: 0.0520 },

    ],
    rig: { neckBase: 'neckBase', neckMid: 'neckMid', head: 'occiput', hip: 'hip', tailBase: 'tailBase' },
    bill: {
      curve: 0,
      // the nail: the tip drops about a millimetre over the last tenth
      hook: 0.001,
      // width from the NHMO skin's top view (narrowest at a fifth, widest 58-74% out at 1.07 times the base), depth
      // from three side views (a quarter of the base's at the nail), both scaled to the live birds' base

        [0, 0.99, 1], [0.2, 0.92, 1], [0.3, 0.95, 0.92], [0.4, 1.01, 0.81], [0.5, 1.01, 0.7], [0.58, 1.07, 0.64],

      ],
    },
    // the photo's eye: 7 mm across with its rim, the iris dark brown
    eye: { radius: 0.003, t: 0.402, iris: '#3b2415' },
    wing: {
      // the tip on the photo's primary tips over the stern, 62 mm above the water; the chord runs forward level with
      // the top of the tertial panel to a wrist hidden in the head's feathers. In front of 29% of the chord the wing
      // is a line with no height: in life the flank and breast feathers cover it, and the builder's wing only follows
      // the body over the first 60% of the spine, so a panel there would stand off the narrowing breast
      wrist: [0.157, 0.0349], droop: -2.5,
      outline: [
        [0, 0], [0.26, 0], [0.29, 0.06], [0.36, 0.08], [0.48, 0.09], [0.63, 0.078], [0.75, 0.093], [0.785, 0.1],
        [0.823, 0.073], [0.862, 0.044], [0.9, 0.016], [0.96, 0.006], [1, 0.003],
      ],
    },
    // raised 6.7 degrees above the spine at the tail base, so the tip lands on the photo's, 36 mm above the water; at
    // 0 the tail follows the spine, which runs nearly level there, and its tip would sit 10 mm lower
    tail: { shape: 'rounded', angle: 6.7 },
    feet: 'webbed',
    // the drake's curl: the two central uppertail coverts, loops about 20 mm high on the rump, top 53 mm above the water
    parts: [{ kind: 'curl', at: 'rear', dx: 0.005, height: 0.019, versions: ['male'] }],
    // where the water meets the bird on the photo's midline, at the stern and the breast (y = 1778)
    float: { waterline: -0.0146 },
    plumage: MALE,
    variants: { male: MALE, maleMoult: MALE_MOULT, female: FEMALE },
    sources: [
      'Söderquist et al. 2014, PLOS ONE 9(12) e115143 (culmen, bill width and depth at the nostrils, live wild birds)',
      'Baird, Brewer & Ridgway 1884, Water Birds of North America vol. 1 pp. 489-493 (tarsus, middle toe, wing; plumage)',
      'Tobias et al. 2022, AVONET (tail, bill width and depth)',
      'Johnsgard 1975 p. 222 and Kortright 1942 p. 383 (masses, for the draft by buoyancy); Guillemain et al. 2001 (eye to bill tip)',
      'Phillips 1923, A Natural History of the Ducks vol. 2 pp. 4-5; Kortright 1942 pp. 149-151 (plumages, soft parts, the curl)',
      'Millais 1902 pp. 19-26; Bent 1923 pp. 40-41; Pyle 2005 (the drakes\' moult out of eclipse, the curl\'s regrowth)',
      'bill top view from NHMO-BI-64916/1-P, Natural History Museum, University of Oslo, image 14188762, CC BY 4.0',
      'profile traced from "Drake Mallard (12972875845).jpg", Stan Bousson / USFWS Midwest, public domain, https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Drake_Mallard_(12972875845).jpg',
      'female checked against "Stockente (19) (34183721334).jpg" (c) Kathy Büscher, CC BY 2.0, https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Stockente_(19)_(34183721334).jpg',
      'female bill and colours from "Stockente (46) (34216838683).jpg" (c) Kathy Büscher, CC BY 2.0, https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Stockente_(46)_(34216838683).jpg',
      'bill and head in side view from "Mallard 2025 11 15 03.jpg" (c) Alexis Lours, CC BY 4.0, https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Mallard_2025_11_15_03.jpg',
      'the moulting drake from iNaturalist 136360466 (c) Fern Sibley Wexler, CC BY-NC, https://www.inaturalist.org/observations/136360466',
      'the moulting drake afloat from iNaturalist 185373900 (c) Zev D., CC BY-NC, https://www.inaturalist.org/observations/185373900',
      'numbers and marks: docs/research/2026-09-25-mallard.md',
    ],
  },
}
```

#### How the Mallard was traced

This is the method for each Part 2 bird. The scripts are in the scratchpad's `plan/mallard-work/`:
- `outline.json`: the hand-read outline
- `opt.mts`: the fit, which writes `trace.json`
- `fit.mts`: the build and the measures
- `st.mts`: photo point to skin (u, t)
- `overlay.py` and `offsets.py`: the traced outline against the photo
- `compare14.py`: the viewer's render against the photo

**The photo and the scale.**
- The profile is traced from Stan Bousson's side-on swimming drake (4296 px), the research's photo 1.
- Its culmen, from where the feathers meet the bill's top to the nail's tip, is 501 px. Scaled to the 53 mm mean of
  the sexes, that is 0.10579 mm per pixel.
- The hip sits at photo (1500, 1640), 14.6 mm above the water.
- The male's photo stands in for the mean-sized bird. A drake's culmen is 55.1 mm, so every length here is 4% under
  his.

**The waterline.**
- The water is the level line through the points where it meets the bird's midline, at the stern (x 590) and the
  breast (x 2960): y = 1778.
- The photo was taken from about 7° above. The near-side waterline therefore dips mid-body, to y ≈ 1848, the same 7–8 mm
  a 65 mm half-beam gives at that angle.
- The research measured its heights against that near-side line, directly below each point: back 77–83, crown 96–107.
  Measured against the midline they are 70.8 and 86.4 on this bird. The female's photo (from about 13° above, a
  15 mm dip) gives back 72 by the midline.
- So the Mallard sits at the low end of section 10's height bounds, and its waterline length (252 mm) at the low end
  of 250–335.
- Buoyancy agrees with the midline: at a 60 mm draft the hull displaces 0.93 L, for birds of 1.03–1.26 kg.
- In the game the player looks down 4–9° at the near water, so the model's near-side waterline dips as the photo's
  does.

**The outline.**
- The outline was read by hand on 1:1 crops of the photo with a 25–100 px grid: 46 points along the top from the stern
  to the bill's base, 15 down the chin, throat, and breast, and 7 under the stern. It is checked by drawing it on the
  photo.
- Under the water the outline is a keel, invented because nothing shows the hull. It runs from where the water meets
  the stern to where it meets the breast, `depth · sin(π ξ^a)^0.7`, deepest (60 mm) 55% of the way forward.
- The depth was chosen so the draft share (0.46) and the body's depth (131 mm, the research's standing drakes 125–131)
  fit.

**The fit.**
- Each station's up and down, the neck and head stations' tilt, and their places were fitted by coordinate descent
  (`opt.mts`). The fit minimises the area between the loft's side view and the outline on a 0.5 mm grid, with the head
  and neck counted double.
- A large penalty holds `worstFold` at 0, and another pulls the eye onto the photo's eye, fitting `eye.t` as well.
- The side view must be the loft's own 64 rings, joined by straight chords. A finely sampled spine hides the chords,
  which cut 5 mm off the breast between two widely fanned neck rings.
- Stations were first placed by hand along a smooth spine, the body level and the neck rising over its last third.
- The neck base sits at the top of the breast's bulge, so the breast counts as body. `breastFrontAbove` reads only rings
  up to the neck base, and with the neck base low it was 5 mm under the water. It is now 9.2 mm, the photo's 9.3.
  The Gadwall's and Wigeon's tests hold `breastFrontAbove`, so their neck bases need the same care.
- Where the spine turns forward into the head, the throat is on the inside of the turn. Folds there are what the tilts
  and the fitted stations remove.

**The bill.**
- The builder's bill is symmetric about its axis, so the axis runs from the photo's tip to the middle of the bill's base
  (half the base depth below the culmen's feathered base). It points 31.7° down.
- The bill points along the spine's last chord, so `lores` sits on that line, 68 px behind the base. The tip lands within
  1.0 mm of the photo's, 1 mm down at the nail from the hook.
- The width and depth profile are section 9's, scaled to a 21 mm base width.

**The wing.**
- The tip is on the photo's primary tips, and the chord runs level with the top of the tertial panel (droop −2.5°).
  The 280 mm chord then puts the wrist inside the head.
- The outline is 0 for the first 29% of the chord, so the wing's front is a line with no area. In life the breast and
  flank feathers cover it. The builder's folded wing hugs the body sections only over the first 60% of the spine
  (u ≤ 0.6), so a panel there would stand 30 mm off the narrowing breast.
- The measured chord is the 3D wrist-to-tip distance. The wrist sits 49 mm further out than the tip, which crosses over
  the rump, so it is 284 mm, 1.5% over, inside `species.test`'s 2%. A wrist further out would fail it.
- The wing's height follows the photo's tertial panel, from the chord down to the chestnut band's lower edge, and the
  tertial flap over the black rump.

**The tail.**
- The tail roots at the tail base's spine point, deep in the stern, 87 mm from the photo's tip.
- `tail.angle` 6.7° turns it from the spine's direction there onto the photo's tip, 36.4 mm above the water.
- This is why the default hangs low. At 0 the tail follows the spine at the tail base, which rises only 8° toward the
  rear, while a floating Mallard carries its tail about 15° up (12–19° on the photos). Its tip would then sit at 26.5 mm.

**The curl.**
- The curl stands at the rear station plus 5 mm, 19 mm high. Its top is 53.1 mm above the water (the photo's 53.7).
- The loft's rump there stands about 3 mm above the photo's, so the loops are a millimetre shorter than the research's
  20 mm.

**The paint.**
- Photo points were carried to skin coordinates with `st.mts`, which finds the ring whose side-view segment holds the
  point: its u, and t from where along the segment it lies.
- At mid-body the water meets the flank at t 0.55–0.59, and at the stern at t 0.72.
- The ring runs obliquely, from u 0.78 at the side of the neck (t 0.35) to u 0.84 at the throat (t 0.82), so it is two
  stripes. The green above it is stripes and fills.
- The saddle on the female's bill is six fills stepped in (s, t). On the bill, t runs from the culmen (0) to the chin (1);
  a mark d mm below the culmen at depth D sits at t = acos(1 − 2d/D)/π.
- Colours are the research's words, checked against the photos in flat light (`mallar3-female-side-2.jpg`), not the warm
  December sun of the relaxed female.

---


# Part 2: the five birds, in parallel

Tasks 15–19 run at the same time, one agent per bird, after Task 14 has landed on the swimmers branch. The user
chose this so every bird gets the same care in a long session.

**How each bird's agent works:**
- **Setup:**
  - Its own worktree off the swimmers branch's head after Task 14: `git worktree add .worktrees/swim-<code> -b swim-<code> <swimmers head>`, then `npm ci` inside it.
  - Never a symlink or junction. Never `git stash`.
  - Its own dev-server port (given per task), with `--strictPort`.
- **Model:** the most capable one. Tracing and painting from photographs is judgment work.
- **Files:**
  - It edits its species file and its test file (given in full below).
  - The only shared files it may touch: `src/birds/detail/fingerprint.test.ts`, adding its own prints; and `src/birds/species/species.test.ts`, only where a test names its species' versions, or to raise the every-version build test's timeout as atlases are added. The controller resolves the merge conflicts these adjacent additions make.
  - It never edits shared code. A bird that needs a builder, painter, behaviour, or viewer change stops and reports what it needs and why. The controller makes that change on the swimmers branch as its own reviewed task, and the bird rebases.
- **Method:** the one in Task 14's "How the Mallard was traced": the research's recommended photos, scaled by the culmen; the standard station labels; `float.waterline` from the photo; `tail.angle` from the photo; each version's paint layers from the research's section 4; the `marks` text rewritten for what the player sees in late September; `sources` crediting every photo and the research document. Tracing photos stay in the scratchpad and are never committed.
  - The Mallard's fitting scripts are in the scratchpad's `plan/mallard-work/`: the outline, the coordinate-descent fit, photo point to skin coordinates, and the overlays. Copy them into the bird's own scratchpad folder and adapt them; never into the repo.
  - **Heights above the water:** the research measured them against the near-side waterline of photos taken from a little above, so its bounds run high. Measured against the midline, as the Mallard is, a bird lands near the low end of section 10's height bounds. If a correctly traced bird falls below a bound for this reason, stop and report the numbers rather than bending the trace; the controller decides whether the bound moves.
  - **The folded wing:** the builder's folded wing follows the body's sections only over the first 60% of the spine. On a duck the wing's front third must have zero height, where the breast and flank feathers cover it, as on the Mallard.
  - **The breast's front:** `breastFrontAbove` reads the loft only up to the neck base. Put the neck base above the breast's bulge, as on the Mallard.
- **Tests first:** it writes its test file, sees it fail because the species has no `detail` block, then traces until the test file and every shared test (`species.test.ts`, the fingerprints, the whole-cast ring budget) pass. The fingerprint test lists species by name: add the bird's prints as Task 14 did for the Mallard.
- **Review and merge:** a reviewer checks each bird against its brief, its test file, and its screenshots. The birds merge into the swimmers branch one at a time, with `npm test && npx tsc --noEmit -p .` after each merge.
- **The cast:** a bird's cast entry changes in Part 3, not here, so the five never edit `data/locations/lake.json` at once.

Each task below gives: the files, the port, the photos and the research sections to work from, the versions, anything
particular to the bird, the test file in full, and the commit.

---

### Task 15: The Gadwall, detailed

**Files:**
- Create: `src/birds/species/gadwall.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/birds/species/gadwal.ts`; `src/birds/detail/fingerprint.test.ts` (its prints, as Task 14 added the Mallard's); `src/birds/species/species.test.ts` only if a test there names its versions or the every-version build needs more time

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `swimmer.testkit.ts` (Task 14); `measure` with the swimming and bill measures, `billWidthAt` (Tasks 2–3); `feet: 'webbed'`, `bill.profile`, `bill.hook`, `float` (Tasks 2–4); the built Mallard (Task 14), for the comparisons.
- Produces: `gadwal` with a `detail` block and versions `male` and `female`.

Port 5211. Research: `docs/research/2026-09-25-gadwall.md`.
- **Photos** (section 8, in the scratchpad's `ref/gadwal/`):
  - `gadwal-m-1.jpg`: the male's profile, 0.178 mm/px
  - `gadwal-f-2.jpg`: the female's profile, 0.202 mm/px
  - `gadwal-f-1.jpg`: plumage and the speculum
  - `gadwal-f-3.jpg`: the waterline
  - `gadwal-m-sept.jpg` and `gadwal-f-local.jpg`: local birds on the game's dates
  - The harvested `gadwal.jpg` is for colour only.
- **Numbers:** section 9.
- **Marks:** section 4, including the female-Mallard differences in section 4.3.
- **Particular to it:**
  - A parallel-sided bill with a round tip (section 2.6).
  - The steeper forehead: 26–47° above the culmen line against the Mallard's 13–19°. It isn't tested; check it in the viewer beside the Mallard.
  - The white speculum patch shows on about two-thirds of floating birds.
  - No curl.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `src/birds/species/gadwall.test.ts`:

```ts
import { SPEC as GADWALL } from './gadwal'
import { SPEC as MALLARD } from './mallar3'
import { billWidthAt } from '../detail/measure'
import { built, atlas, within, draftShare, hueIn } from './swimmer.testkit'

// Bounds from docs/research/2026-09-25-gadwall.md, section 10 (Phillips, Ridgway, AVONET, photo-measured), in mm. The
// Mallard comparisons use the built Mallard (Task 14), both at rest.

const G = built(GADWALL, 'female'), Gm = built(GADWALL, 'male')
const M = built(MALLARD, 'female')
const widest = (b: typeof G) => Math.max(...Array.from({ length: 101 }, (_, i) => billWidthAt(b.model, i / 100)))

describe('Gadwall: structure', () => {
  it('builds both versions, the same bird', () => {
    expect(Gm.M.length).toBeCloseTo(G.M.length, 6)
  })
  it('culmen, bill depth and width at the base', () => {
    within(G.M.culmen, 37, 45)
    within(billWidthAt(G.model, 0) * 1000, 13.5, 19)
    within((billWidthAt(G.model, 0) * 1000) / G.M.culmen, 0.35, 0.46)
  })
  it('a parallel-sided bill with a round tip: even at 30%, 50% and 90% of the culmen from the tip, narrower at 7%', () => {
    const w = [0.7, 0.5, 0.1].map(s => billWidthAt(G.model, s))
    const lo = Math.min(...w), hi = Math.max(...w)
    expect(hi / lo).toBeLessThanOrEqual(1.08)
    within(billWidthAt(G.model, 0.93) / widest(G), 0.55, 0.75)
  })
  it('tarsus, wing chord, tail, and length at rest', () => {
    within(G.M.tarsus, 33, 44)
    within(G.M.wingChord, 234, 280)
    within(G.M.tail, 70, 95)
    within(G.M.length, 290, 370)
  })
  it('floats at its measured heights, with about 0.38-0.55 of its depth under the water', () => {
    within(G.M.backAbove, 45, 78)
    within(G.M.crownAbove, 85, 130)
    within(G.M.tailTipAbove, 5, 60)
    within(G.M.breastFrontAbove, 0, 22)
    within(draftShare(G.M), 0.38, 0.55)
  })
  it('against the Mallard: smaller, with a shorter, narrower bill', () => {
    within(G.M.culmen / M.M.culmen, 0.68, 0.82)
    within(billWidthAt(G.model, 0) / billWidthAt(M.model, 0), 0.70, 0.88)
    within(G.M.length / M.M.length, 0.85, 0.95)
    within((G.M.culmen / G.M.length) / (M.M.culmen / M.M.length), 0.75, 0.92)
  })
})

describe('Gadwall: plumage', () => {
  const f = atlas(GADWALL, 'female'), m = atlas(GADWALL, 'male')

  it("female bill: dark along the culmen from base to tip, with no orange break, over orange lower sides", () => {
    const panel = f.colour('bill', [0.05, 0.8], [0.6, 0.9])
    expect(hueIn(panel.hue, 12, 40)).toBe(true)
    expect(panel.sat).toBeGreaterThan(0.4)
    for (let s = 0.05; s < 0.95; s += 0.05) {
      expect(f.stats('bill', [s, s + 0.05], [0, 0.12]).mean, `culmen at ${s.toFixed(2)}`).toBeLessThanOrEqual(0.5 * panel.lum)
    }
  })
  it('the white speculum patch shows on the folded wing, in both versions', () => {
    for (const a of [f, m]) expect(a.share('wingFold', [0, 1], [0, 1], c => c.lum > 0.85)).toBeGreaterThan(0.02)
  })
  it('male: a grey, low-saturation body and a black stern', () => {
    expect(m.colour('skin', [m.u('hip'), m.u('breast')], [0.45, 0.75]).sat).toBeLessThan(0.15)
    expect(m.stats('skin', [m.u('rear'), m.u('tailBase')], [0, 1]).mean).toBeLessThan(0.15)
  })
  it('female: browner flanks than the male\'s', () => {
    expect(f.colour('skin', [f.u('hip'), f.u('breast')], [0.45, 0.75]).sat)
      .toBeGreaterThan(m.colour('skin', [m.u('hip'), m.u('breast')], [0.45, 0.75]).sat + 0.05)
  })
})
```

### Task 16: The American Wigeon, detailed

**Files:**
- Create: `src/birds/species/wigeon.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/birds/species/amewig.ts`; `src/birds/detail/fingerprint.test.ts` (its prints, as Task 14 added the Mallard's); `src/birds/species/species.test.ts` only if a test there names its versions or the every-version build needs more time

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: as Task 15.
- Produces: `amewig` with a `detail` block and versions `female`, `eclipse`, and `male`.

Port 5212. Research: `docs/research/2026-09-25-american-wigeon.md`.
- **Photos** (section 8, `ref/amewig/`):
  - `amewig-male.jpg`: the body profile and waterline, 0.20 mm/px
  - `amewig-female.jpg`: 0.177 mm/px
  - `amewig-eclipse-feeding.jpg`: the eclipse colours and the feeding pose
  - `amewig-eclipse-forewing.jpg`: the white forewing on the water, and the bill's depth profile
  - The harvested photo is head-on: body width and colour only.
- **Numbers:** section 9.
- **Marks:** section 4.
- **Particular to it:**
  - A short, parallel-sided, blue-grey bill with a black tip and a black nail 8–10 mm long.
  - A round head.
  - A wedge tail raised 17–33°, ending about 30 mm past the wingtips.
  - The `eclipse` drake's chestnut flanks and white forewing bar.
  - The `female` version also stands for juveniles and eclipse drakes with the wing hidden.
- **Versions:** the block `variants` gain `eclipse`. The base layer list is the female's; `detail.variants` holds `eclipse` and `male`.

```ts
import { SPEC as WIGEON } from './amewig'
import { billWidthAt, billDepthAt } from '../detail/measure'
import { built, atlas, within, draftShare, hueIn } from './swimmer.testkit'

// Bounds from docs/research/2026-09-25-american-wigeon.md, section 10 (Baird, Brewer & Ridgway; Grinnell et al.;
// Phillips; AVONET; photo-measured), in mm.

const W = built(WIGEON, 'female')
const widest = Math.max(...Array.from({ length: 101 }, (_, i) => billWidthAt(W.model, i / 100)))

describe('American Wigeon: structure', () => {
  it('builds all three versions, the same bird', () => {
    for (const v of ['eclipse', 'male']) expect(built(WIGEON, v).M.length).toBeCloseTo(W.M.length, 6)
  })
  it('a short bill: culmen, width and depth at the base', () => {
    within(W.M.culmen, 31, 40)
    within(billWidthAt(W.model, 0) * 1000, 14.5, 17.5)
    within(billDepthAt(W.model, 0) * 1000, 10.5, 14.5)
    within((billWidthAt(W.model, 0) * 1000) / W.M.culmen, 0.38, 0.52)
  })
  it('parallel-sided with a round tip, widest outside the last 30% (unlike the Shoveler)', () => {
    expect(billWidthAt(W.model, 0.7) / widest).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0.9)
    expect(billWidthAt(W.model, 0.4) / widest).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0.97)
    within(billWidthAt(W.model, 0.95) / widest, 0.35, 0.65)
    expect(W.M.billWidestAt).toBeLessThanOrEqual(0.7)
  })
  it('tarsus, wing chord, tail, length at rest, and the tail well past the wingtips', () => {
    within(W.M.tarsus, 34, 42)
    within(W.M.wingChord, 228, 273)
    within(W.M.tail, 78, 112)
    within(W.M.length, 300, 360)
    within(-W.M.projection, 10, 45)
  })
  it('floats at its measured heights, the breast front at the waterline', () => {
    within(W.M.backAbove, 45, 70)
    within(W.M.tailTipAbove, 35, 65)
    within(W.M.breastFrontAbove, 0, 10)
    within(draftShare(W.M), 0.4, 0.5)
  })
})

describe('American Wigeon: plumage', () => {
  const f = atlas(WIGEON, 'female'), e = atlas(WIGEON, 'eclipse'), m = atlas(WIGEON, 'male')
  const flank = (a: typeof f) => a.colour('skin', [a.u('hip'), a.u('breast')], [0.45, 0.75])

  it('every version: a pale blue-grey bill with a black tip', () => {
    for (const a of [f, e, m]) {
      const base = a.colour('bill', [0.1, 0.6], [0.3, 0.7])
      expect(base.lum).toBeGreaterThan(0.4)
      expect(hueIn(base.hue, 190, 235)).toBe(true)
      expect(a.stats('bill', [0.8, 1], [0, 1]).mean).toBeLessThan(0.2)
    }
  })
  it('male: a crown lighter than the cheek, a green patch behind the eye, pinkish flanks', () => {
    expect(m.stats('skin', [m.u('occiput'), m.u('lores')], [0, 0.15]).mean)
      .toBeGreaterThan(m.stats('skin', [m.u('occiput'), m.u('eye')], [0.45, 0.65]).mean + 0.15)
    expect(m.share('skin', [m.u('occiput'), m.u('eye')], [0.2, 0.45], c => hueIn(c.hue, 90, 170) && c.sat > 0.25)).toBeGreaterThan(0.2)
    expect(hueIn(flank(m).hue, 330, 30)).toBe(true)
  })
  it('female: a greyer head than her orange-rufous flanks', () => {
    const head = f.colour('skin', [f.u('nape'), f.u('lores')], [0.2, 0.7])
    expect(head.sat).toBeLessThan(flank(f).sat - 0.1)
    expect(hueIn(flank(f).hue, 15, 40)).toBe(true)
  })
  it("eclipse: redder, more saturated flanks than the female's, and a white bar on the folded wing's upper edge", () => {
    expect(flank(e).sat).toBeGreaterThan(flank(f).sat)
    expect(e.share('wingFold', [0, 0.6], [0, 0.35], c => c.lum > 0.85)).toBeGreaterThan(0.1)
    expect(f.share('wingFold', [0, 0.6], [0, 0.35], c => c.lum > 0.85)).toBeLessThan(0.05)
  })
})
```

### Task 17: The Northern Shoveler, detailed

**Files:**
- Create: `src/birds/species/shoveler.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/birds/species/norsho.ts`; `src/birds/detail/fingerprint.test.ts` (its prints, as Task 14 added the Mallard's); `src/birds/species/species.test.ts` only if a test there names its versions or the every-version build needs more time

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: as Task 15, plus the spoon bill from `bill.profile` (Task 3).
- Produces: `norsho` with a `detail` block and versions `eclipse` and `female`.

Port 5213. Research: `docs/research/2026-09-25-northern-shoveler.md`.
- **Photos** (section 8, `ref/norsho/`):
  - `norsho-female-side-2.jpg`: the body, head and bill profile, 0.298 mm/px
  - `norsho-eclipse-side.jpg`: the eclipse drake's plumage, 0.238 mm/px
  - `norsho-female-side.jpg`: the alert profile
  - `norsho-pair.jpg`: both in the same light
  - `norsho-bill-skull.jpg` (12.82 px/mm, ruler) and `norsho-bill-top.jpg`/`-2.jpg`: the width profile
  - The harvested photo is a breeding drake in flight, not usable.
- **Numbers:** section 9, including its bill table of (s, width, depth).
- **Marks:** section 4.
- **Particular to it:**
  - The bill's `profile` comes straight from the section 9 table: widths and depths divided by the base's 16.5 and 21 mm.
  - It sits low in front, the back rising about 8° toward the tail.
  - Every late-September drake is in eclipse: yellow to orange-yellow eye, blackish bill, greyish head, rufous scalloped flanks, and a white patch on the upper rear flank.
- **Versions:** replace the block `variants`' `male` with `eclipse`, since no breeding drake appears in late September and the guide's swatches should show the eclipse drake. The base layer list is the female's; `detail.variants` holds `eclipse`.

Create `src/birds/species/shoveler.test.ts`:

```ts
import { SPEC as SHOVELER } from './norsho'
import { billWidthAt, billDepthAt } from '../detail/measure'
import { built, atlas, within, draftShare, hueIn } from './swimmer.testkit'

// Bounds from docs/research/2026-09-25-northern-shoveler.md, section 10 (Phillips, Johnsgard, Baird et al., AVONET,
// four bill outlines, photo-measured), in mm. Both versions are late-September birds: the drake in eclipse.

const S = built(SHOVELER, 'eclipse')
const widths = Array.from({ length: 101 }, (_, i) => billWidthAt(S.model, i / 100))
const widest = Math.max(...widths)

describe('Northern Shoveler: structure', () => {
  it('builds both versions, the same bird', () => {
    expect(built(SHOVELER, 'female').M.length).toBeCloseTo(S.M.length, 6)
  })
  it('a long bill, distinctly longer than the head', () => {
    within(S.M.culmen, 58, 74)
    within(S.M.culmen / S.M.head, 1.2, 1.6)
  })
  it('the spoon: 1.5-2.1 times as wide at its widest, 65-80% of the way out, as at the base', () => {
    within(billWidthAt(S.model, 0) * 1000, 14, 19)
    within(widest * 1000, 27, 32)
    within(S.M.billWidestRatio, 1.5, 2.1)
    within(S.M.billWidestAt, 0.65, 0.8)
    within(billWidthAt(S.model, 0.5) / widest, 0.78, 0.95)
    within(billWidthAt(S.model, 0.93) / widest, 0.55, 0.85)
  })
  it('widens steadily from the base to its widest point, with no waist', () => {
    const top = widths.indexOf(widest)
    let low = widths[0]!
    for (let i = 1; i <= top; i++) {
      expect(widths[i]!, `at ${i}%`).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(low - 0.05 * widths[0]!)
      low = Math.max(low, widths[i]!)
    }
  })
  it('deep at the base and thin at the tip, much wider than deep at its widest', () => {
    within(billDepthAt(S.model, 0) * 1000, 17, 23)
    within(billDepthAt(S.model, 0.9) * 1000, 5, 9)
    expect(widest / billDepthAt(S.model, S.M.billWidestAt)).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1.8)
    within(S.M.billTipRise, -1.5, 1.5)
  })
  it('tarsus, wing chord, tail, length at rest, and the tail past the wingtips', () => {
    within(S.M.tarsus, 32, 40)
    within(S.M.wingChord, 215, 250)
    within(S.M.tail, 64, 95)
    within(S.M.length, 280, 360)
    within(-S.M.projection, 15, 45)
  })
  it('floats at its measured heights', () => {
    within(S.M.backAbove, 35, 60)
    within(S.M.crownAbove, 75, 100)
    within(S.M.tailTipAbove, 15, 50)
    within(draftShare(S.M), 0.38, 0.55)
  })
})

describe('Northern Shoveler: plumage (late September: no green head, no white breast)', () => {
  const e = atlas(SHOVELER, 'eclipse'), f = atlas(SHOVELER, 'female')
  const flank = (a: typeof e) => a.colour('skin', [a.u('hip'), a.u('breast')], [0.45, 0.75])

  it("the eclipse drake's iris is yellow; the female's is dark", () => {
    const iris = e.colour('eye', [0, 1], [0, 1])
    expect(hueIn(iris.hue, 38, 58)).toBe(true)
    expect(iris.sat).toBeGreaterThan(0.5)
    expect(f.stats('eye', [0, 1], [0, 1]).mean).toBeLessThan(0.2)
  })
  it("the drake's bill is darker; the female's has orange lower sides", () => {
    expect(e.stats('bill', [0, 1], [0, 1]).mean).toBeLessThan(f.stats('bill', [0, 1], [0, 1]).mean)
    const side = f.colour('bill', [0.1, 0.9], [0.6, 0.95])
    expect(hueIn(side.hue, 18, 38)).toBe(true)
    expect(side.sat).toBeGreaterThan(0.4)
  })
  it("the drake's flanks are redder than the female's buff, with a brighter patch on the rear flank", () => {
    expect(flank(e).hue).toBeLessThan(flank(f).hue)
    expect(e.stats('skin', [e.u('tailBase'), e.u('hip')], [0.4, 0.6]).mean)
      .toBeGreaterThan(e.stats('skin', [e.u('hip'), e.u('breast')], [0.4, 0.6]).mean + 0.1)
  })
  it('neither version has a green head or a white breast', () => {
    for (const a of [e, f]) {
      expect(a.share('skin', [a.u('occiput'), a.u('lores')], [0, 0.7], c => hueIn(c.hue, 90, 170) && c.sat > 0.3)).toBeLessThan(0.05)
      expect(a.stats('skin', [a.u('breast'), a.u('neckBase')], [0.5, 1]).mean).toBeLessThan(0.8)
    }
  })
})
```

### Task 18: The American Coot, detailed

**Files:**
- Create: `src/birds/species/coot.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/birds/species/amecoo.ts`; `src/birds/detail/fingerprint.test.ts` (its prints, as Task 14 added the Mallard's); `src/birds/species/species.test.ts` only if a test there names its versions or the every-version build needs more time

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: as Task 15, plus `bill.shield` and the `shield` part (Task 3), `feet: 'lobed'` and `lobeWidth` (Task 4).
- Produces: `y00475` with a `detail` block and versions `adult` and `juvenile`.

Port 5214. Research: `docs/research/2026-09-25-american-coot.md`.
- **Photos** (section 8, `ref/amecoo/`):
  - `amecoo-side-oct-reago.jpg`: the floating profile and waterline, 0.248 mm/px
  - `amecoo-side-adult-wildreturn.jpg`: bill and shield, 0.109 mm/px
  - `amecoo-head-cook.jpg`
  - `amecoo-juv-local-williams.jpg` and `amecoo-juv-local-haight.jpg`: the juvenile
  - `amecoo-pair-local-rakestraw.jpg`: an adult and a near-adult
  - `amecoo-feet-hansen.jpg`: the lobes
- **Numbers:** section 9.
- **Marks:** section 4.
- **Particular to it:**
  - Scale by the bill **without** its shield, 31.8 mm; with the shield would be 25–40% wrong.
  - A narrow, pointed bill, taller than wide.
  - The shield about 9 × 7 mm, with a 4 × 5 mm red-brown callus on the adult (paint).
  - Lobed toes; the middle toe's width across its lobes is 8–12 mm.
  - It floats higher than the ducks.
- **The shield seam:** section A's decisions (in the appendix) say the shield shows a seam unless the `billBase` station's `up` matches half the bill's depth. Follow that.
- **Versions:** the block `variants` gain `adult` and `juvenile`. The shield's geometry is shared; the juvenile's smaller, paler shield is paint.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `src/birds/species/coot.test.ts`:

```ts
import { SPEC as COOT } from './amecoo'
import { billWidthAt, billDepthAt } from '../detail/measure'
import { built, atlas, within, draftShare, hueIn } from './swimmer.testkit'

// Bounds from docs/research/2026-09-25-american-coot.md, section 10 (Ridgway & Friedmann 1941, Eddleman & Knopf 1985,
// Gullion 1951, photo-measured), in mm, at rest with the waterline at y = 0. The shield's geometry is shared by both
// versions; the juvenile's smaller, paler shield is paint.

const A = built(COOT, 'adult')

describe('American Coot: structure', () => {
  it('builds both versions, the same bird', () => {
    expect(built(COOT, 'juvenile').M.length).toBeCloseTo(A.M.length, 6)
  })
  it('a narrow, pointed bill, taller than wide, drooping slightly at the tip', () => {
    within(A.M.culmen, 29, 35)
    within(billDepthAt(A.model, 0) * 1000, 13.5, 17.5)
    within(billWidthAt(A.model, 0) * 1000, 9, 13)
    within(billWidthAt(A.model, 0.5) / billDepthAt(A.model, 0.5), 0.45, 0.75)
    within(A.M.billTipRise, -2.5, 0)
    within(A.M.culmen / A.M.head, 0.85, 1.2)
  })
  it('a small autumn shield up the forehead', () => {
    const sh = A.model.anatomy.shield!
    expect(sh).not.toBeNull()
    within(sh.top.distanceTo(sh.base) * 1000, 3, 12)
  })
  it('length at rest, tarsus, middle toe, wing, and tail', () => {
    within(A.M.length, 285, 340)
    within(A.M.tarsus, 47, 64)
    within(A.M.wingChord, 156, 210)
    within(A.M.tail, 42, 65)
    within(A.M.tail / A.M.wingChord, 0.24, 0.33)
  })
  it('floats higher than the ducks, at its measured heights', () => {
    within(A.M.backAbove, 28, 48)
    within(A.M.tailTipAbove, 0, 25)
    within(A.M.crownAbove, 95, 140)
    within(A.M.draft, 35, 50)
    within(draftShare(A.M), 0.45, 0.6)
  })
})

describe('American Coot: plumage', () => {
  const a = atlas(COOT, 'adult'), j = atlas(COOT, 'juvenile')
  const head = (x: typeof a) => x.stats('skin', [x.u('neckMid'), x.u('lores')], [0, 0.6]).mean
  const body = (x: typeof a) => x.stats('skin', [x.u('hip'), x.u('breast')], [0.1, 0.6]).mean

  it("the adult's head is clearly darker than its body; the juvenile's is nearly the body's, with a paler throat", () => {
    expect(head(a)).toBeLessThan(body(a) - 0.05)
    expect(Math.abs(head(j) - body(j))).toBeLessThan(0.08)
    expect(j.stats('skin', [j.u('neckMid'), j.u('eye')], [0.7, 1]).mean).toBeGreaterThan(head(j) + 0.1)
  })
  it("the adult's bill is near white with a dark band near the tip; the juvenile's is grey, with a weak band or none", () => {
    expect(a.stats('bill', [0.05, 0.6], [0, 1]).mean).toBeGreaterThan(0.75)
    expect(a.stats('bill', [0.7, 0.93], [0, 1]).mean).toBeLessThan(a.stats('bill', [0.05, 0.6], [0, 1]).mean - 0.25)
    expect(j.stats('bill', [0.05, 0.6], [0, 1]).mean).toBeLessThan(a.stats('bill', [0.05, 0.6], [0, 1]).mean - 0.15)
  })
  it('the adult carries a dark red-brown callus on its shield; the juvenile does not', () => {
    const callus = a.colour('shield', [0.6, 1], [0, 1])
    expect(hueIn(callus.hue, 340, 30)).toBe(true)
    expect(callus.lum).toBeLessThan(0.35)
    expect(j.share('shield', [0, 1], [0, 1], c => hueIn(c.hue, 340, 30) && c.sat > 0.35 && c.lum < 0.35)).toBeLessThan(0.05)
  })
  it("the adult's iris is red; the juvenile's brown", () => {
    const ai = a.colour('eye', [0, 1], [0, 1]), ji = j.colour('eye', [0, 1], [0, 1])
    expect(hueIn(ai.hue, 345, 15) && ai.sat > 0.5).toBe(true)
    expect(hueIn(ji.hue, 15, 40)).toBe(true)
  })
  it('both show white undertail wedges, bright against the tail and flanks', () => {
    for (const x of [a, j]) expect(x.share('skin', [x.u('rear'), x.u('tailBase')], [0.7, 1], c => c.lum > 0.85)).toBeGreaterThan(0.3)
  })
})
```

### Task 19: The Pied-billed Grebe, detailed

**Files:**
- Create: `src/birds/species/grebe.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: as Task 15, plus `tail.shape: 'none'` (Task 4), `feet: 'lobed'`, and `bill.hook`.
- Produces: `pibgre` with a `detail` block and versions `basic` and `juvenile`.

Port 5215. Research: `docs/research/2026-09-25-pied-billed-grebe.md`.
- **Photos** (section 8, `ref/pibgre/`):
  - `pibgre-basic-side.jpg`: the profile and bill, 0.150 mm/px
  - `pibgre-juv-side.jpg`: the juvenile, 0.194 mm/px
  - `pibgre-juv-ccby.jpg` and the rest for posture and colour
  - The harvested photo is a breeding adult: good only for the bill's ring position, which isn't built.
- **Numbers:** section 9.
- **Marks:** section 4.
- **Particular to it:**
  - A short, deep bill whose top arches down to a 1–1.5 mm hook. Its depth falls to 0.94, 0.81, 0.61, and 0.36 of the base at 25, 50, 75, and 90% of its length.
  - No visible tail: the white stern puff is the loft's rear (`tail.shape: 'none'`).
  - It rides low: the back about 28 mm above the water when sleek.
  - Lobed toes set far back.
  - The juvenile's striped face.
- **Versions:** the block `variants` gain `basic` and `juvenile`.

```ts
import { loadLocation } from '../location'
import { expandCast } from './spawn'
import { castAtlases } from './paint/prepaint'
import { makeRng } from '../catalog/rng'

// The lake's swimmers in the plumages the research's late-September tallies give (each species' research document,
// section 3): moulting and eclipse drakes, females and female-types, first-autumn coots, and striped grebes.
const MIX: Record<string, number> = {
  'mallar3:male': 1, 'mallar3:maleMoult': 1, 'mallar3:female': 2,
  'gadwal:male': 1, 'gadwal:female': 2,
  'amewig:female': 3, 'amewig:eclipse': 1, 'amewig:male': 1,
  'norsho:eclipse': 2, 'norsho:female': 1,
  'y00475:adult': 4, 'y00475:juvenile': 2,
  'pibgre:basic': 2, 'pibgre:juvenile': 1,
}

describe("the lake's late-September mix", () => {
  const lake = loadLocation()
  it('every seed spawns exactly the same mix of plumage versions', () => {
    for (const seed of [1, 2, 3]) {
      const got: Record<string, number> = {}
      for (const ind of expandCast(lake, makeRng(seed))) {
        const k = `${ind.species}:${ind.variant ?? ''}`
        got[k] = (got[k] ?? 0) + 1
      }
      for (const [k, n] of Object.entries(MIX)) expect(got[k], `${k}, seed ${seed}`).toBe(n)
    }
  })
  it('paints twenty atlases: the six shorebirds and fourteen swimmer versions', () => {
    expect(castAtlases(lake).length).toBe(20)
  })
})
```

2. Whether the ducks sit at the right depth and read as their species and sex: the drake Mallard's curl, the female Mallard's bill against the female Gadwall's, the Wigeon's eclipse drake, the Shovelers' eclipse drakes with yellow eyes.
3. Whether each style reads as its species: Mallards upending, Gadwalls dipping, Shovelers skimming and spinning, Coots pumping and diving, grebes rolling under and surfacing elsewhere.
4. Whether any birds overlap or snap from one pose to the next.

Whatever the user says is the acceptance result. Record it in the final report, with the screenshots from Tasks 14–19.
