# Appendix: decisions and notes from the sections

## Decisions made while planning (section A)

- **The fingerprint** (Task 2, written first against master). A 32-bit FNV-1a over the rest mesh (positions, UVs,
  skin weights and indices, triangles) and, through twelve poses, the matrices of the 18 bones master has and the bill
  tip, every float rounded to 1 µm; plus (Task 3) a print of every shipped atlas's texels. The pose list carries no
  `tailTilt` (Task 4 turns its sign; no behaviour sets it on a shipped bird) and hashes a fixed list of master's 18
  bones (Task 4 appends a `curl` bone, which has no skin on a shipped bird). The one knowing change is the dowitcher's
  three prints in Task 5.
- **The float pivot is the middle of the waterline**: the plane y = waterline cuts the loft; the pivot is midway
  between the cut's rearmost and frontmost points (the stern and the breast at the water). It lies on the water, so a
  tip-up of 1.5 rad lifts the stern by almost half the waterline length: 0.11 m on the test duck, and 0.14 m for the
  Mallard's 0.28 m waterline, inside the spec's 0.10–0.15 m (`docs/research/2026-09-25-mallard.md`, sections 6.3 and
  11). The whole bird is shifted so the pivot is the group's origin; root and body bones sit on it.
- **A swimmer's legs at rest**: the tibia straight down from the hip (so the bare tibia below the belly is exactly
  `measurements.tibia`, which the species test checks), the tarsus and toes trailing back 22.4° below level. The legs
  bone follows the body's lean (its quaternion and its position about the pivot), because for standing birds it hangs
  from root to keep planted feet still. While hidden (scale 1e-4) the legs gather a quarter of the way from the belly
  to the hip: inside the body and under the water, as the grebe's and Coot's research tests ask ("no leg or foot
  vertex above the water while floating", `docs/research/2026-09-25-pied-billed-grebe.md` section 10,
  `docs/research/2026-09-25-american-coot.md` section 10).
- **Paddling**: any `paddle` above 0 shows the legs at full size, and the stroke is `paddle` × 0.5 rad on both the
  tibia and the tarsus, the left leg opposite the right. The legs appear under the water, where the opaque surface
  hides the switch. The viewer strokes at 2.4 a second (`docs/research/2026-09-25-mallard.md`, sections 9 and 11).
- **`reachDepth` and `reachAngle`**: a negative depth counts as 0 (the contract's "0 = the surface itself"), the angle
  is clamped to 0..π/2, and both are applied per call inside `reachDown`, which computes the same doubles as before
  with the defaults, so every existing peck is bit-identical.
- **The floating measures** are taken on the loft at rest. `breastFrontAbove` is the frontmost loft point among the
  rings from the tail to the neck base that lies below the neck base's spine point: the forward curve of the breast
  (the head never counts). On the test duck it is 18 mm above the water (Mallard 0–16 mm, section 6.1).
- **A positive `tailTilt` lifts the detailed builder's tail** (Task 4). It lowered it before, against `PoseState`'s
  doc and the contract ("`tailTilt` raises the tail"); no behaviour gave a shipped detailed bird a tail tilt, so no
  shipped bird changes. (The block builder keeps its own convention; it is not section A's.)
- **Bill profile**: `taper` becomes optional and a bill needs one of `taper` and `profile`. With a profile, the bill
  gets a ring at every profile point, so the mesh passes through the profile exactly; the tip itself is a point, so a
  profile's depth at s = 1 (the Shoveler's 2.5 mm nail) is not built. `billWidthAt` and `billDepthAt` cut the bill
  square to its base direction at a fraction of its length along that direction (not of the chord, which a hook
  lengthens). The widest point is the middle of the stretch within 0.1 µm of the widest (the Mallard's 0.58–0.74
  plateau reads 0.66).
- **Hook**: the centreline drops by `hook` × q² over the last tenth (q from 0 to 1), with four extra rings there.
  Research: a duck's nail about 1 mm, the grebe's 1–1.5 mm (spec; `docs/research/2026-09-25-pied-billed-grebe.md`
  section 9).
- **Shield**: a closed lens on the head's top outline, from the bill base back `length` along it, `width` across,
  bulging `thickness` × √(1−b²) × sin(πa)^0.6 and tucking 0.3 mm under the skin at its rim, on the head bone. Its
  parameters, and the test's, are the Coot's late-September shield: 9 mm, 7 mm, 2.2 mm
  (`docs/research/2026-09-25-american-coot.md`, sections 5.2 and 9). The callus is paint: a layer on `part: 'shield'`
  with `s` about [0.55, 1]. For the Coot's agent: set the `billBase` station's `up` to half the bill depth, so the shield
  runs straight on from the culmen; if the head's skin shows between bill and shield, paint the skin's top at the bill
  base white (the test duck's stand-in showed a 1.5 mm dark seam, its station being 1.25 mm taller than its bill).
- **New atlas regions in free space**: `shield` at (448, 384, 64 × 32) and `curl` at (256, 416, 64 × 32). The atlas
  print proves every shipped atlas unchanged. The painter's `partFrames` gains both frames, and a profiled bill's frame
  follows its profile (a tapered bill's is computed exactly as before).
- **Webbed feet**: a closed lens 0.6 mm thick between each pair of neighbouring front toes, in the toes' plane, its
  free edge dipping back 15% between the tips; the hind toe stays plain ("small, raised and unlobed",
  `docs/research/2026-09-25-mallard.md` section 5).
- **Lobed feet**: a closed lens either side of each toe segment, rounded and notched to nothing at the joints: 2, 3,
  and 4 segments on the inner, middle, and outer toes (`docs/research/2026-09-25-american-coot.md`, sections 5.5 and 9;
  the counts beyond the middle toe's three are its estimate). `lobeWidth` is the middle toe's width across its lobes:
  the Coot's 0.010 m (section 9); the grebe's research estimates 0.3 of the toe, about 0.013 m
  (`docs/research/2026-09-25-pied-billed-grebe.md`, section 9). Without it, 0.2 of the toe.
- **`tail.shape: 'none'`**: no tail mesh; the tail bone sits at the tail base; the loft's rings from the rear end to
  the tail base hand off linearly from the tail bone to the body, so `tailTilt` cocks the stern; `anatomy.tailTip` is
  the rearmost loft point, so `length` runs bill tip to stern (the grebe's research measures "bill tip to the end of
  the stern tuft"). The species test skips the tail-length check for a tailless bird (`measurements.tail` stays, for
  the record) and skips the walking-feet check for swimmers.
- **Version-only parts**: `detail.parts`, at most one of each kind, each on a bone of its own named after the kind
  (only `curl` so far), appended to `BONE_NAMES` so no skin index moves; `versions` must be keys of `detail.variants`;
  a bird with no version, or another version, hides the part (scale 1e-4, set once after binding). The part is built
  on every bird of the species, so every version has the same mesh. It is painted as the part `'curl'`, in its own
  atlas region: the drake's layers fill it black. The curl is two closed tubes 1.2 mm thick, 3.2 mm apart, each turning
  300°: up and back from the back, over, and down in front, `height` high (`docs/research/2026-09-25-mallard.md`,
  sections 4.1, 5, and 9: two black loops about 20 mm high, their top about 57 mm above the water).
- **Tilt** is interpolated along the spine like the other section sizes, and turns a section's plane about the
  across axis: positive swings its lower side forward. `worstFold` now measures a fold against each ring's own plane;
  untilted, that is the spine's tangent exactly, so every shipped bird's crease check reads as before. The fixture test
  (a deep throat under the head's turn, 1.8 mm creased square, 0 with the crown tilted 15° and the eye 10°) passes
  under either measure; the retraced dowitcher needs the plane measure (see Task 5 for the independent check).
- **The dowitcher's throat** (Task 5): searched, not hand-set, because the throat, the chin, the dorsal line, the eye,
  and the head's length all pull on the same seven stations; the search's limits are in Task 5. The notch is gone:
  the front outline is within 1.8 mm of the photo from the breast to the chin (it was 8.3 mm short). The spec's
  fallback (the notch as a known soft spot) is not needed.
- **The viewer**: float mode is the game's lake, opaque water at y = 0 with the ground dropped 1 m out of sight; `sink`
  lowers both birds; `lean` reaches 1.6 rad; new field views `bino15` and `scope35` match the spec's field views
  (binoculars at 15 m for the near water, the telescope at 30–40 m for the open water).
- **Found, not fixed:**
  - Under the dowitcher's chin the outline has a small concave corner where the throat meets the bill's underside (as
    the old trace did); the chin sits 1.1–1.8 mm behind the photo's.
  - The dowitcher's culmen/head is now 1.81 (was 1.89): tilting the occiput's section lengthens the back of the head
    by 1.5 mm. It is inside the test's 1.7–2.4 and the brief's 1.8–2.0, near the lower edge.
  - A swimmer's paddling legs are thin tubes that trail straight back from the hips; seen from the side in a tip-up,
    the webs are nearly edge-on. The spec asks only that the feet show and paddle.
  - A profiled bill shows faint shading bands at its rings in close-ups.
  - No detailed swimmer species exists until Task 14; the swimming builder was checked on the test duck and two
    scratch stand-ins, not on a traced bird.

## Decisions made while planning (section B)

Research documents are `docs/research/2026-09-25-<species>.md`; "§11" is a document's section 11. "Ours" marks a
value no source gives.

**The cast (Task 1)**
- **Counts are whole numbers, at least 1.** A zero count would be a version named for no bird. The schema rejects a
  non-integer, zero, negative, or non-numeric count. `validateCast` checks the names and the sum, and checks the
  counts again for a location built in code, which skips the schema.
- **`validateCast` takes the species specs as a third argument,** defaulting to `SPECIES`. Tests pass stand-ins,
  because no species has a swim style yet.
- **The spawner deals a record's versions out shuffled** with its own rng. A cast without records draws exactly the
  numbers it did, so the lake spawns as before.
- **A flyover's record** is an error in `validateCast`, and gives the flock no version rather than crashing.

**Styles and the shared body (Task 7)**
- **Two style types, `ProbeStyle` and `SwimStyle`, with `FORAGING` their union.** A swim style on a prober feeds as a
  sandpiper, a probe style on a swimmer swims as a dabbler, and `validateCast` rejects both.
- **`mudflat.test.ts` loops only over probing species.** It isn't in section B's file list, but it indexes
  `PECK_BOTTOM` by every detailed species' style. That no longer type-checks, and would crash on the detailed
  Mallard in Task 14. The change is three `continue` lines and an import.
- **`createFlock` moved to `src/behavior/flock.ts` unchanged,** its outside-the-zone rule included. `between` and
  `ease` moved to `types.ts`, beside a new `wrap`.
- **Swim flocks start formed.** The spawner seeds a swimming flock's target at a point in its zone, not at the
  vantage, and keeps it there for the flock's first `retargetSeconds`. Its members start within the flock's radius,
  clear of the swimmers already placed (`startSpot`). Seeded at the vantage, every duck would spend its first minute
  swimming to its flock, and 24 wakes at once would overflow the ring buffer.
- **Every flock's target drifts at most 3 m a minute,** and a bird farther than twice the flock's radius swims back to
  it. Ours: no source gives either.
- **Spacing, two numbers a style: `flock` between flockmates, `other` from anyone else.**
  - A pair that aren't flockmates keeps the larger of their two `other` spacings, so each keeps at least its own.
  - The soft rule: inside the spacing plus 0.1 m, the part of the motion toward the neighbour is taken out and the
    aim is steered round it.
  - The hard rule: no step ever goes further inside a neighbour's spacing.
  - A bird that starts too close paddles away at up to 0.12 m/s (ours).
  - The tests allow 1 cm: in the scratch runs no pair started apart ever went inside at all.
- **Spacings, by style:**
  - Flockmates: Mallard 0.4 m, over its 0.36 m traced length (mallard §11: nearest neighbour 0.3–2 m); Gadwall and
    Wigeon 0.35 m, over their 0.325 and 0.33 m lengths (gadwall §7.6: a pair 0.1–0.45 m; american-wigeon §11:
    0.3–1.0 m); Shoveler 0.25 m, the spinning pair's distance (northern-shoveler §11: tandem 0.2–0.35 m); Coot 0.4 m
    (american-coot §11: 0.4–0.9 m); grebes 10 m (spec, pied-billed-grebe §11).
  - From anyone else: 0.8 m for the ducks and the Coot, after the Gadwall's unpaired birds' 0.3–1.2 m (gadwall
    §7.6); 1.5 m for a grebe (ours: "ignores ducks except to swim away from them").
  - At 0.4 m or more no two ducks overlap however they face. The Shoveler's 0.25 m lets a pair nose to tail touch
    bill to tail, as they do in a tandem skim.
- **The shared body never uses the zone clamp.** A step that would leave the zone is halved until it stays in. A
  swimmer pivots to turn about: its speed is scaled by `max(0, cos(off))`, and it gathers way at 1 m/s² (ours).
- **Neighbours are the controllers themselves.** The spawner hands each swimmer the list of swimmer controllers in
  its zone, and a bird tells itself apart by identity. `BehaviorController` gains `spacing` and `group` so a
  neighbour can be read without a wrapper.
- **The first style's shares are the Mallard's** (mallard §11, Gray et al. 1996): cruise 0.311, loaf 0.068, preen
  0.065, alert 0.057. Task 8 adds the feeding share.

**`dabbler` (Task 8)**
- **A species picks its settings from `DABBLERS`, keyed by species code** (`mallar3`, `gadwal`, `amewig`). Any other
  dabbler, or a bird with no species, gets `MALLARD`. `speciesTraits` (Task 7) hands every behaviour its species'
  code, so the spawner and `species.test.ts`'s behaviour test both give a Gadwall its own settings.
- **What to do next is chosen by `pickByShare`:** weight = share / mean length, so each activity's long-run time
  share is its research share. The shares need not sum to 1: the Wigeon's §11 budget sums to 1.045, and each share is
  its fraction of the sum.
- **The feeding split is by bout.** A tip-up bout's level spells count as tip-up time, and a head-dip bout's pauses
  as head-dip time, as Szijj 1965 counted birds by how they were feeding (mallard §7.2). Every bout opens with a
  pause, so two tip-ups never run together.
- **Ranges resolved** (each value inside the research's range):
  - **Mallard** (mallard §11):
    - Tip-up 2–6 s, uniform, mean 4 (range 1.5–6, mean 4). 2–4 in a bout, 1–5 s level between.
    - 85° (80–90°) = 1.48 rad. Over in 0.35 s (0.2–0.5), back in 0.25 s (0.2–0.3). Tail cocked 0.4 rad at full tilt
      (ours).
    - Head-dips 1–3 s (0.5–3, mean about 2). 2–6 in a bout, 0.5–1.5 s between (the Gadwall's 1 s up, gadwall §11).
      The body tipped 7.5° (0–15°), the tail up 10° = 0.17 rad.
    - Surface dabbling 5–25 s at 0.1 m/s (0.05–0.15).
    - Flock radius 2.5 m (2–3).
  - **Gadwall** (gadwall §11):
    - Cruise 0.40 m/s. Shares feed 0.45, swim 0.20, loaf 0.20, preen 0.05, alert 0.10. Split surface 0.50, head-dip
      0.38, tip-up 0.12.
    - Tip-up 1.7–4.5 s, mean 3.1 (1.1–4.5, mean 3.1). 5–15 in a bout, 2–4 s between. 85°. 2.5 strokes a second.
    - Head-dips 1–3 s, 3–8 in a bout, the body tipped 20° (10–30°), the tail not raised.
    - Surface 2–10 s at 0.05 m/s. Loaf 10–60 s, alert 1–3 s. Flock radius 1.5 m (ours, for a pair and one).
  - **Wigeon** (american-wigeon §11):
    - Cruise 0.4 m/s; cruising 0.175 (15–20%).
    - Tip-ups 8% of feeding (at most 10%). The rest split evenly between surface picking and head-dip bouts, which the
      research doesn't divide (ours).
    - Dips 1–2 s with 3–6 s of picking between: one about every 6 s, Knapton & Knudsen's 10.5 a minute. 3–8 dips in a
      bout (ours).
    - Tip-up 2–4 s at 80° (70–90°) = 1.40 rad, two or three together (ours), 2.5 strokes a second (the Gadwall's).
    - Surface picking 5–20 s at 0.15 m/s (0.1–0.2), the body 7.5° (5–10°). Flock radius 1.5 m (ours).
  - **All three:**
    - A head-dip takes the head 0.15 m under, where the Gadwall's head-dips find plants 10–30 cm down (gadwall §7.3).
    - A dabbling bill is at 0.5 rad (about 30°) to the surface, from the Wigeon's measured surface-picking pose.
    - The head takes 0.3 s to go under or come up (ours).
- **Ring sizes are ours** (a size scales a ring's spread, life, and brightness): a tip-up 0.7 at each end, a head-dip
  0.5 at each end, 0.18 m ahead of the body, a dabbling bill 0.3 every 1–2 s (mallard §11).

**`shoveler` (Task 9)** (northern-shoveler §11 and §7.3; every number there is from video)
- **Shares:** skim 0.38, circle 0.05, cruise 0.22, and the loaf's 0.34 split as rest 0.18, preen 0.14, and alert
  0.02, from Webb & Brotherson's resting 21.9% and preening 16.9%, with the scans of Guillemain et al. 2001.
- **The tip-up is a rate, not a share.** §11 gives it both a 1% share and "about one per 10 minutes of feeding",
  which disagree about fivefold. The plan uses the rate: 1/600 per second of skimming. It lasts 1–2.5 s at 55°
  (50–60°) = 0.96 rad.
- **Skim:**
  - Half travelling, 0.32 m/s with the head down 30–60 s, then up 2 s.
  - Half pottering at 0.03 m/s, the face down 0.75–2.75 s and up 0.25–1.0 s, in bouts of 20–60 s (ours).
  - The bill at 27° (20–35°) = 0.47 rad. The travelling bill 0.008 m into the surface film (the contract's
    0.005–0.01); the potterer's face 0.035 m under, to the eye (the eye is 3–4 cm up in a skim).
  - The yaw wander is ±6.5° (5–8°) at 0.45 Hz (0.3–0.6), shown on the heading only, never steering the path.
  - The head eases in 0.2 s, so a 0.25 s raise still shows (ours).
- **Circle:**
  - 0.25 m apart, 3.4 s a revolution, one direction a bout. Bouts of 4–8 s, and one in ten (ours) 60–120 s.
  - A partner anywhere in the flock's 3 m that is skimming, loafing, preening, or looking about (ours).
  - The pair gathers at 0.15 m/s and gives up after 12 s (ours).
  - A bird asks for a spin at half the circle's share, since every spin gives two birds their circling. One flock of
    three circled 6.6% of an hour; the test holds four flock-hours to 2–10%.
- **Cruise** 0.45 m/s (0.36–0.6), the old swim's. Its wake only above 0.35 m/s, so a travelling skim leaves its own
  0.5 ring every 1.5 s instead (ours). A pottering dip rings 0.3, a spin 0.6 at the start and once a revolution, a
  tip-up 0.6 at each end (all ours).

**`coot` (Task 10)** (american-coot §11 and §7)
- **Time:**
  - Travel 0.15 at 0.45 m/s (0.4–0.5); loaf 0.10 for 10–60 s; preen 0.10 for 5–20 s.
  - Peck bouts 2–8 s at 2.5 a second. The bill held at the water half of each peck, 0.2 s (0.15–0.4); raised 0.3 of
    its reach between, about 2.5 cm of 60–95 mm.
  - Peck bouts are separated by 1–4 s (the video's 1–5 s of "swimming or looking up"), swimming three times in four.
    With the dives' 5% taken out, that gives pecks 38% (the budget's 40%) and the feeding swim 14% (15%) of the time.
    A pause of 1–5 s, swimming 60% of it, gave pecks only 36%.
- **The looking-up pause is the `alert` state,** with a 0.5 s flick of the tail to 0.65 rad (37.5° of 30–45°). The
  alarm that holds the tail up for 1–5 s is out of scope; nothing in the game alarms a coot.
- **Head pump** 2.2 a second (1.8–2.6): held still against the water for half of each pump, then thrust along
  smoothstep. 0.17 body lengths from end to end, and the video's 2–3 cm lift isn't a pose field. It shrinks with the
  speed below the feeding swim's 0.2 m/s, and stops when the bird does.
- **Head-dip** 0.8–1.5 s, one peck bout in 3.5 (1 in 3–4); the head 0.1 m under (ours), the body 7.5° (5–10°). That
  makes 2.5% of the time where the budget's line says 5%; the rate from the video was kept.
- **Tip-up** 1–2 s at 67.5° (45–90°), the tail 0.5 rad; 1/300 per second of pecking or swimming to feed, about once
  in 9 minutes overall (once per bird in 5–10 minutes).
- **Dives:**
  - Bouts at 1/450 per second (once in 5–10 minutes), 2–5 dives, 10–25 s after each surfacing.
  - Under 1.3 + 4.2 u^2.5 s, whose mean is 2.5 (1.3–5.5); moving u² m, mean 0.33, three in four under 0.5 m (0–1 m,
    mostly under 0.5).
  - The dip 0.2–0.3 s and the lunge 0.5 s (video). The lunge arcs the body to 80° with the feet and tail up.
  - Ours: 0.4 m takes the whole bird under; up back first in 0.3 s, the nose a fifth of the way down; eating 2–5 s on
    the surface; swimming at most 1 m/s under water.
  - Halfway down, it moves its surfacing spot if another bird is floating on it.
- **Rings (ours):** a dive 0.55 going down and coming up (a ring of 0.5–1 m), a head-dip 0.4 at each end, the pump
  0.3 every 1.5 s (1–2 s).

**`grebe` (Task 11)** (pied-billed-grebe §11, §6, §7)
- **Under water** a gamma-distributed 7.6 s (sd 3.1, kept to 2–15). Travelling a gamma-distributed 3.7 m (sd 3.0,
  kept to 0–12): Bleich 1975, drawn with Marsaglia and Tsang's method, within 60° of its heading.
  - A long way takes the time it takes at 1.5 m/s (ours; forage dives net 0.5 m/s, escape dives 1.1).
  - The whole way if the zone lets it: other ways round are tried before the zone's edge cuts a dive short. Cut
    short at once, with the grebes wandering off their patches to the edge, the mean distance was 1.6 m.
- **Grebes keep 10 m apart, and more at the start.**
  - Each works a patch 6 m either way from where it started (ours), and starts at least 20 m from the other grebes:
    "tens of metres apart". With 8 m patches and 10 m starts, grebes came up beside each other.
  - It chooses where to come up with a metre to spare. When its time is up it checks every frame, and swims on for up
    to 5 s more (ours) to a clear spot.
  - Measured: no dive over 15.25 s and no pair on the water closer than 10 m.
- **Dive starts:**
  - Roll-under 70%: gone 0.4–0.65 s after the face goes in, the stern up 25° (20–30°) = 0.44 rad, a kick of the feet.
  - Splash 15%: the puff up 0.2–0.3 s, then straight down in 0.1 s.
  - Sink 15%: down to back-awash in 0.4 s, held 1.5 s, then under in 0.2 s (ours; the video's head went down in
    about 0.07 s).
- **Depths:** `AWASH` 0.073 m, the back from +0.028 m (§6.2's model back height) to −0.045 m (§6.3). 0.4 m all the
  way under (ours).
- **Surfacing:** up in 0.1 s, the neck upright by 0.4 s. The pause 2–10 s with a mean of 4 (Bleich's ratio gives 3.3),
  drawn skewed toward the short end, a third of dives preceded by a 0.8–1.2 s peer.
- **The rest:**
  - Bouts are half its time, of 3–12 dives. The rest of the time is split cruise 0.15 and loaf 0.27 (ours).
  - Cruises of 5–30 s at 0.15 m/s over 1–5 m (ours), a 0.4 ring every 1.5 s.
  - Loafs of 30–120 s, the stern puff cocked 30° (20–40°).
  - Sink-and-watch once in 4.5 minutes (3–6): settling in 1–3 s, holding 5–30 s, then rising in 2–4 s or, half the
    time (ours), sliding under into a bout.
  - Measured: 35% of its time under water (the research's "about 35%").
- **Not modelled:** a fish or crayfish handled on surfacing (15%); surfacing out of sight (5%; the lake's open water
  has no emergent plants); the fluffed loafing bird riding 20 mm higher. A grebe's `y` stays 0 afloat, as the
  contract has it.

**Diving, targeting, and rings (Task 12)**
- **`submerged` means all the way under:** `y` at −0.4 m, from the end of the lunge or entry until the bird starts
  up. The spawner sets `model.group.visible` from it every frame.
- **A bird under the water is no one's neighbour:** a duck swims over a diving coot. A diver choosing where to come
  up counts the birds under the water too (`clearAt(…, under)`), since they are about to come up themselves.
- **`targetCandidates` lives in a new file, `src/player/candidates.ts`.** It isn't in section B's file list, but it
  is a new file that no other section touches, and `main.tsx` can't be imported in a node test. `main.tsx` changes
  only its import and the candidates line.
- **The identification drop needed no code.** A diving bird leaves the candidates, so it leaves the target as if it
  had swum out of the reticle. The test runs the main loop's two lines around a coot that dives and comes back.
- **The ring budget is met by rates, and `MAX_RIPPLES` stays 32.**
  - The wake every 1.5 s instead of 0.9 s: the grebe research's measured wake rhythm (pied-billed-grebe §11). The
    Mallard research gives no rate; "ripples come from the wake, not from each stroke" (mallard §11).
  - A dabbling bill's ring 0.3 instead of 0.4, and the Coot's pump ring only while it swims to feed.
  - Measured: 14–16 live rings on average, 28–32 at the peaks, nothing pushed out. With the old wake the wakes alone
    reached 29, and about 290 rings in 20 minutes were pushed out, half of them wakes.
  - The peaks come near 32. If the cast grows, the next step is a larger buffer: `MAX_RIPPLES` 48 had no evictions
    even at the old rates (demand peaked at 38). Its cost in the water shader, 48 rings looped per fragment against
    32, can only be measured in the browser on the user's GPU.
- **The six species files don't name their styles in this section** (not in its file list). `cast.test.ts` mocks
  `getSpec` to give them their styles; a spec that already names one keeps it, so the mock is harmless once Task 14
  and Part 2 set them.

**Found, not fixed**
- The block models read `lean.x` as a roll (`procedural.ts`), so until the six birds are detailed a tip-up rolls a
  block duck onto its side, and the preening roll and the Coot's lean pitch it a little.
- `species.test.ts` drives each detailed species under its cast behaviour, with `speciesTraits` and no neighbours:
  a detailed swimmer there swims as a lone bird in its own style and settings. Nothing in this section needs
  neighbours to run.

## Decisions made while planning (section C)

None of these rests on a research number. The only numbers the task uses are the spec's own (section "Painting in a Web Worker": 6 atlases now, 20 after the round, 57–65 ms each).

- **Which atlases the cast needs: every version of every detailed species in the cast.**
  - `castAtlases` walks the birds `expandCast` makes. For each bird of a species with a `detail` block, it adds all of the species' versions if the bird drew one, or the species' own plumage if it has none. Flyovers are block flocks and need none.
  - The result doesn't depend on the spawner's seed or on how a `'mixed'` entry or a Task 1 count record is dealt out, so no drawn version can be missed.
  - It goes through `expandCast`, not the cast entries, so `pickVariant`'s rules stay in one place. One such rule: a named plumage the species lacks means its own plumage.
  - On the current cast this gives the 6 shorebirds, one atlas each. With the six swimmers detailed it gives 6 + 3 + 2 + 3 + 2 + 2 + 2 = 20, the spec's count.
- **The cast is built only after every atlas is ready.** `spawnCast` runs in `prepaint(...).then(...)`, and Begin enables after it. Building birds early and painting them lazily was rejected: it would add a half-built state, and birds would pop in.
  - `materialFor` keeps its synchronous paint on a cache miss, for the viewer and the tests. At startup it never misses, because `prepaint`, even on its fallback path, has filled the cache before any bird is built.
  - No detailed bird can fall back to blocks: `createBirdModel` chooses by `spec.detail`, as before.
- **One worker, every job posted at once.** Replies are matched by `atlasKey`, progress is reported after each, and the worker is terminated at the end.
  - Paint time is measured in the worker, around `paintSpecies`. `storeAtlas` adds it to `paintStats` on the main thread, so the overlay's "ms each" is the worker's paint time.
  - Main-thread painting counts itself through `paintAtlas`, as before, so nothing is counted twice.
- **The fallback.** Two failures send the rest of the atlases to the main thread:
  - the factory throws (no `Worker`);
  - the worker fires `error`: its script failed to load, or a paint threw.

  The fallback paints each atlas through `materialFor`, one per macrotask, so the card keeps redrawing, and warns once in the console. If even that throws, or `spawnCast` does, the card shows "The birds could not be set up, so the lake cannot start. The credits still work." in place of Begin, like the no-WebGL message. Before, a throw at that point left a blank page.
- **`new Worker(new URL(…))` lives in `main.tsx`,** passed into `prepaint` as a factory.
  - This keeps the pattern Vite rewrites out of every module the worker itself imports; the worker imports `painter.ts`.
  - It also lets the tests hand `prepaint` a fake.
- **The handler is split from the worker entry.** `job.ts` is a plain function node can test. `paint.worker.ts` is the 4-line entry that registers it.
  - The contract names "a new worker file". This is that file plus two small modules beside it (`job.ts`, `prepaint.ts`), all new, in `src/birds/paint/`.
- **Progress lives in its own store.** `createStore<PaintProgress>` in `main.tsx` is passed to `App`, which subscribes with `useStore`. It is not a new field in `game.ts`, which is not among section C's files.
- **The progress line.** It reads "Painting the birds… 7 of 20" under a greyed, disabled Begin.
  - Once ready, it is hidden (`visibility: hidden`), not removed. The card is centred on the screen, and removing the line moved Begin about 10 px just as it enabled; the first screenshot pass showed it.
  - `onBegin` also checks `ready`, as a second guard.

## Known soft spots (section C)

- **Paint time varies between page loads,** 62–276 ms per atlas for the same build in headless Chromium on this machine.
  - Reloads in the same browser context ran at 198–276 ms. Fresh contexts ran at 91–201 ms, and first loads in a tab at 64–65 ms.
  - The machine was at about 60% load from other agents, and its CPU is hybrid (10 cores, 12 threads), so a worker may land on a slow core.
  - Neither the worker nor the minifier is the cause. The dowitcher round saw 208–213 ms on the main thread. In node, the minified worker bundle painted at 52 ms and the unminified one at 60.
  - At 20 atlases, one worker means about 1.3–5.5 s behind the title card, which stays responsive. If Phase 3's performance reading finds the wait long, the next step is two workers, by calling the factory twice and splitting the jobs.
- **The no-Worker fallback is tested in node** (a factory that throws, no factory, and a worker that errors part-way), but was not forced in a browser.
- **The worker validates the species registry again** (zod) when it loads. The first atlas arrives 250 ms to 1 s after the card appears, against 40–320 ms between later atlases.

## Review focus (section C)

1. **A browser with no module workers, or a worker script that 404s.** It must fall back to painting on the main thread, and Begin must still enable: `prepaint.test.ts` "paints on the main thread when a worker can't be made" and "keeps what the worker painted before it failed …".
2. **A `'mixed'` entry, or a count record, for a detailed species.** Every version it could deal must be painted: "covers every detailed bird the cast can make, whatever the seed" (20 seeds), and "paints every version of a detailed species with versions …".
3. **Building a bird after startup.** It must paint nothing and add nothing to `paintStats`: "leaves materialFor nothing to paint …".
4. **Begin must not move or fire while painting:** `Title.test.tsx`, and Begin's bounding box in the browser check.

## How section C was checked

- **Every block ran.** Every code block above ran in a scratch worktree of `c588f75` with the `PoseState` precondition commit: 609 → 620 → 623 tests, `tsc` clean at each commit, and `npm run build` green.
- **It applies on top of the other sections.** In a throwaway worktree, both of this task's commits cherry-picked cleanly onto a merge of section A's Tasks 2–4 and section B's Tasks 1 and 7, as they stood. There, `npm run build` was green with 694 tests.
- **Note for the controller: never use `git stash` in a worktree.** `refs/stash` is shared by every worktree of the repo.
  - Sections B and C each stashed within seconds of each other, and each `git stash pop` took the other's entry.
  - Both agents recovered their work, and section C restored its own by stash SHA, without a pop.
  - The leftover entries were dropped, and the shared stash list is empty.
  - The rescue notes are in `plan/rescue/README.txt`.
  - Worth adding to the contract's worktree rules.
